## Unified Aicadia Studio direction selected

- **Status:** selected direction; implementation pending explicit plan acceptance.
- **Choice:** remove Atlas, Workbench and Observatory as selectable product modes and
  build one coherent Aicadia Studio. Atlas supplies the source-first foundation;
  one dedicated model inspector makes current domain meaning, realized PostgreSQL
  columns and exact foreign-key relations legible; one substantially clearer Live
  section makes bounded World records and their relations directly navigable. Every
  supported source, model, MCP tool, storage object and live record receives a stable
  deep link and copyable development reference for use in AI conversations.
- **Schema tooling:** add an explicit Rust-produced, downloadable schema snapshot of
  the connected PostgreSQL structure, including tables, columns, constraints,
  foreign keys and indexes plus a structural fingerprint. It captures no World rows,
  writes no database or repository state and never becomes a parallel schema
  authority; retained history or diffing remains deferred until a concrete need
  earns it.
- **Reason:** observed use made Atlas the clearest base, while a permanent variant
  chooser obscures rather than supports daily development. A single navigable
  environment must remain useful as repository activity, model complexity and World
  data grow, without duplicating the authorities it projects.
- **Affected scope:** `studio/src/`, `web/`, the local Studio operation contract,
  development-interface concept and later delivery evidence. No game schema, World
  mutation, OpenAPI/MCP capability, Agent contract, authentication or durable
  multiple-World identity changes.

## Unified Aicadia Studio completed

- **Status:** completed; one Studio is the current development interface.
- **Choice:** replace the comparison chooser with one accessible Atlas-derived
  Game/Live application. Game directly navigates owning sources, structured
  model/storage relations, the exact MCP catalog and development states. Live links
  bounded Entity, Character, Place, Activity and PostgreSQL structure views. Stable
  query links and copyable references preserve the selected context for AI
  conversations.

  `public` tables, columns, constraints, ordered foreign keys and indexes, returns a

  schema artifact and writes no snapshot or database state.

  interface experiments and keeping mechanically available truth generated from its
  owning source.

  PostgreSQL/browser inspection, 161 passing Rust tests, strict Clippy/formatting,
  syntax/integrity checks and the disposable launcher lifecycle.
- **Affected scope:** local development projection, browser presentation, operation
  contract and evidence only. Game schema, World behavior, public HTTP/MCP, Agent
  text, authentication, token spend and durable multiple-World identity remain
  unchanged.

## Studio as the seamless development environment — proposed

  delivered unified Studio does not yet give the overview needed to step back and

  repository upkeep. The draft plan
  `dev/plans/20260817-161615-studio-development-environment/plan.md` proposes a
  convention-driven Studio that owns no content or allowlist, one folder per model
  under `game/docs/model/<model>/`, one home per volatile record field in front
  matter, `cargo test` lint over the same parsers, four-section path-routed
  navigation, expanded bounded Live reads and a Markdown builder brief. Ten material
  questions (D1–D10) remain open in the plan; nothing is accepted yet. See the
  updated [Studio concept](../aicadia-studio.md).
- decided / D1 Studio rendering: on 2026-08-17 the User chose Rust server-rendered
  pages with one stable path route per Studio resource over extending the
  just-delivered browser application. Reason: reload, history, sharing and copying
  work without browser state, every page is provable in `cargo test`, one resource
  costs one handler plus template, and Rust keeps all application logic. The JSON
  Studio API and the SPA route/state machine are removed once replaced. Affected
  scope: `studio/src/`, `web/`, Studio tests and the local Studio operation

- decided / D3 model folders: on 2026-08-17 the User accepted one folder per model

# 2026-08-18

## Passwordless local PostgreSQL startup — planned

- user direction / local launcher: on 2026-08-18 the User required plain
  `cargo dev` to start without asking for a PostgreSQL password. Direct diagnosis
  found that OrbStack owns conventional port 5432 with password authentication,
  while the one started Homebrew `postgresql@18` service accepts the local User
  without a password on its configured port 5433. The proposed bounded repair keeps
  explicit `DATABASE_URL` precedence, otherwise selects only a reachable
  conventional or configured started Homebrew service non-interactively, and never
  scans arbitrary ports or stores credentials. The preserved `aicadia_local`
  database has exactly the original User while its moved private profile is absent;
  the plan recovers that profile without database mutation rather than weakening
  missing-profile safety. Affected scope: local launcher operation, its tests and
  local-play documentation/evidence; no World, schema, HTTP, MCP, Agent or Studio
  capability behavior.

## Spatial multiplayer foundation — root boundary accepted

- accepted / typed truths over unified change: on 2026-08-18 the User accepted
  typed structural truths over one unified World-change substrate as the root
  spatial direction. Immediate placement, containment or attachment, Place
  topology, fictional ownership, actual User-control/access and operation-specific
  remote links retain separate game meanings and invariants; bounded Actions may
  compose their exact facts and settle current state, one Activity and dirty
  resources through the same change path. Reason: open-ended exploration and
  creation must support a birdhouse placed on a table in a village as well as a
  button linked to a remote bomb without making `inside`, `owns`, `may_control` and
  `triggers` synonyms or separate mutation authorities. Physical storage, the first
  relation catalogue, the first spatial game scene and every runtime contract remain
  open for the continuing research and one-question grill. Affected scope: live
  spatial concept and research interpretation only; no `game/docs/`, schema,
  capability, implementation or active plan changed.

## Spatial multiplayer foundation — Entity/structure boundary corrected

- accepted / Connections and frames are not Entities: on 2026-08-18 the User
  rejected modeling either a Connection or SpatialFrame as an Entity role. An
  Entity is a thing or concept that is part of the World; a Connection is typed
  structural truth between World subjects and a spatial frame is coordinate or
  transform structure used by a Place or moving Entity. A physical door, path,
  road, portal or ship may itself be an Entity and may ground or be referenced by
  the corresponding structure, but that does not turn the Connection or frame into
  the same Entity or a second Entity. Reason: structural machinery must not pollute
  the game subject model or make internal coordinate/traversal records appear as
  discoverable World things. Stable internal keys and Activity-backed history remain
  possible without Entity identity. Affected scope: live spatial concept and later
  model/schema constraints; no current `game/docs/`, schema, capability or runtime
  behavior changed.

## Development Areas — flat current-truth work documents

- accepted / flat Area set: on 2026-08-18 the User accepted a deliberately flat
  first set of six Development Areas: `Multiplayer`, `Place`, `Movement`,
  `Discovery`, `Agent Play` and `World Change`. Overlap is allowed because an Area
  is a navigation and working lens rather than exclusive domain ownership; for
  example one accepted movement can appear through Place structure, Movement
  transition, World Change settlement and Multiplayer concurrency without copying
  their underlying facts. `Exploration` is not a separate first Area because it is
  the player experience composed especially from Place, Movement and Discovery.
  The Place route remains singular as `/dev/areas/place`. Affected scope: Area
  information architecture and future Studio projection; no game behavior,
  planning order, schema or public Agent surface changes.
- accepted / current development synthesis: on 2026-08-18 the User required each
  Area to become a living work document that shows the current truth around its
  subject: meaning, explicit `is` and `is not` boundaries, chosen and rejected
  directions, still-unselected questions, needed research, components/concepts and
  the current technical model. Studio must render those distinctions visibly rather
  than presenting another long undifferentiated document. The Area owns that
  current development synthesis; exact executable behavior remains in `game/docs/`,
  sourced findings in Research, experiment verdicts in Lab, delivery claims in
  Evidence and the selected question, plan and execution state in Work. Durable
  unchosen and research-needed landscape may appear in the Area without making it
  current Work. Affected scope: documentation home boundaries, current concept
  migration, Area source shape and Studio Area UI; no World, PostgreSQL, HTTP, MCP
  or Agent behavior.

## Spatial multiplayer foundation — first scene selected

- accepted / connected-Place exploration first: on 2026-08-18 the User selected a
  second Place being discovered or established and a Character moving to it through
  explicit connectedness as the first concrete spatial multiplayer scene. This
  orders exploration and movement ahead of nested Entity placement and a remote
  button-to-bomb link, without rejecting either later requirement. Reason: the scene
  advances Aicadia's core exploration outcome while forcing the smallest useful set
  of Place identity, placement, connectedness, movement, authority, concurrency and
  history decisions. The exact identity rule, discovery flow, connection shape,
  movement timing, schema and capability remain open for the one-question grill.
  Affected scope: current Place and Movement Area synthesis plus retained spatial
  rationale; no `game/docs/`, runtime, database, Agent surface, backlog ordering or
  active-plan semantics changed.

## Spatial multiplayer foundation — first Place identity boundary

- accepted / no inferred discovery merge: on 2026-08-18 the User chose that the
  first spatial slice does not recognize or merge two independently confirmed Place
  discoveries merely because they appear similar. Each discovery not structurally
  proven to identify one known Place receives and keeps its own stable identity;
  names and descriptions never prove sameness. A later explicit evidence and
  reconciliation behavior remains open. Reason: the World cannot infer geographic
  identity, while stable independent identities keep concurrent discovery, retry
  and Activity history deterministic without global matching or rewriting. Affected
  scope: current Place and Discovery Area synthesis plus retained spatial rationale;
  no current Discovery contract, `game/docs/`, schema, runtime, public capability or
  backlog state changed.

## Spatial multiplayer foundation — discovery does not imply entry

- accepted / discover before deliberate entry: on 2026-08-18 the User chose that
  discovering the selected second Place establishes that destination and its
  explicit connectedness while the Character remains at the origin. The player may
  inspect the discovery and choose not to enter, matching ordinary real-world
  exploration. Entering is a later deliberate Movement action and separately
  settled change with its own Activity; discovery never changes Character placement
  implicitly. Reason: discovery changes what has become established in the shared
  World, while Movement changes where the Character is, and the player retains
  control over both. Affected scope: current Place, Discovery, Movement and World
  Change Area synthesis plus retained spatial rationale; no `game/docs/`, schema,
  runtime capability, public Agent text, backlog ordering or active plan changed.

## Spatial multiplayer foundation — explicit connection direction

- accepted / no inferred return path: on 2026-08-18 the User chose that each
  connection explicitly states which direction or directions it allows. A
  connection can support A to B, B to A or both; the existence of forward travel
  never causes World to infer a return path. This leaves access, physical grounding
  and current traversability separate and still open. Reason: cliffs, currents,
  portals and other one-way spatial facts must fit without exceptions or a second
  model, while ordinary two-way paths can name both directions explicitly. Affected
  scope: current Place, Movement and Discovery Area synthesis plus retained spatial
  rationale; no `game/docs/`, schema, runtime capability, Agent text, backlog or
  active plan changed.

## Spatial multiplayer foundation — Entity, Connection and Route vocabulary

- accepted / physical thing versus travel structure: on 2026-08-18 the User
  sharpened the spatial vocabulary after rejecting wording that blurred direct
  connectedness and a Route. A tangible door, bridge or road is an Entity because
  it is a thing in the World. A Connection is the direct non-Entity structural fact
  between two Places, and a Route is a separate non-Entity ordered travel course
  through one or more Connections. A physical Entity may participate in or affect
  travel without becoming the Connection or Route itself. Reason: stable Entity
  identity belongs only to World subjects, while traversal structure must remain
  composable without manufacturing World things. Whether an open-terrain Connection
  may omit a physical Entity and whether Routes are ever durable remain open.
  Affected scope: canonical vocabulary, current Place and Movement Area synthesis
  and retained spatial rationale; no `game/docs/`, schema, runtime capability,
  public Agent text, backlog or active plan changed.

## Spatial multiplayer foundation — separate Route concept reopened

- corrected / Route was recorded too strongly: later on 2026-08-18 the User
  questioned whether Connection and Route need to be distinct at all and asked what
  independent value Route would add. Current truth therefore keeps only the settled
  boundary: a door, bridge or road is an Entity, while a direct Connection is not.
  Route is removed from canonical vocabulary and chosen Area direction until the
  grill decides whether it is merely an on-demand ordered selection of Connections
  or deserves separate durable World state. Reason: a term or model earns its place
  only through behavior a Connection alone cannot express. Affected scope:
  canonical vocabulary, Movement Area and retained spatial rationale; the preceding
  entry remains decision history, and no `game/docs/`, schema, runtime capability,
  Agent text, backlog or active plan changed.

## Spatial multiplayer foundation — Route deferred until it has gameplay

- accepted / no stored Route in the first slice: on 2026-08-18 the User chose that
  World initially stores only direct Connections. An Agent may select one or more
  Connections as a temporary travel plan, and one Connection may constitute the
  complete chosen journey, without creating Route state. A separate Route concept
  may be introduced later when it gains concrete behavior, exemplified by a
  Character deciding to take the named Green Route to City C. Its later naming,
  saving, sharing, progress and lifecycle remain undecided. Reason: Connection alone
  serves the selected first exploration scene, while Route should earn storage only
  when a reusable journey becomes gameplay. Affected scope: current Movement Area
  synthesis and retained spatial rationale; canonical vocabulary correctly remains
  without Route, and no `game/docs/`, schema, runtime capability, public Agent text,
  backlog or active plan changed.

## Spatial multiplayer foundation — exploration-world granularity researched

- researched / no new spatial choice: on 2026-08-18 a fresh comparison of
  Civilization VII and the open Civ-family implementation Unciv, Minecraft Bedrock,
  OpenMW, Evennia and Generative Agents/Smallville was recorded in
  [Spatial granularity in exploration worlds](../../research/exploration-world-spatial-granularity.md).
  Tile and continuous-world games keep exact spatial ground separate from
  settlements, terrain regions and technical partitions; a room/graph world remains
  coherent by deliberately omitting the in-between unless another Room or coordinate
  layer is added. The research therefore rejects no accepted Area direction and
  selects no tile, cell, coordinate, patch, terrain or schema model. It sharpens the
  next grill boundary to one player promise: whether a Character may persistently
  stop, meet, discover or place an Entity at an unnamed point between established
  Places without first establishing another Place. Affected scope: research trail
  and ongoing spatial grill only; no `dev/areas/`, `game/docs/`, schema, runtime,
  Agent surface, backlog or plan changed.

## Spatial multiplayer foundation — persistent position between Places

- accepted / Place is not the complete spatial ground: on 2026-08-18 the User chose
  that a Character may persistently stop at an unnamed point between established
  Places, meet another Character there, discover there or place an Entity there
  without first establishing that point as another Place. This rejects a pure
  Place-graph model as Aicadia's complete long-term spatial foundation and requires
  a separate non-Entity exact placement beneath or between Places. The choice does
  not yet name that placement, choose cells or coordinates, define Place, change the
  selected first second-Place scene or amend delivered behavior. Reason: arbitrary
  in-between play is part of the intended exploration and creation promise, while
  manufacturing a Place for every stopping point would disguise a lower spatial
  layer as World subjects. Affected scope: canonical Character and Entity-placement
  wording, current Place, Movement, Discovery, Multiplayer and World Change Area
  synthesis, and retained spatial rationale; no `game/docs/`, schema, runtime,
  public Agent surface, backlog or plan changed.

## Spatial multiplayer foundation — Entity and Place boundary reopened and researched

- researched / no new Entity or Place choice: on 2026-08-18 the User challenged
  both the object-like intuition around Entity and the earlier shorthand that Place
  is a durable “where,” while explicitly remaining open to terminology familiar
  from game frameworks. The User proposed relationship or Interaction participation
  as an Entity characteristic. Fresh comparison of Bevy, Unity Entities, Unreal,
  Godot, Evennia, OpenMW, Civilization VII and Overture Maps was recorded in
  [Entity and Place through a game-framework lens](../../research/entity-place-framework-boundary.md).
  The research recommends a broad stable Entity identity for independently
  addressable fictional World subjects, with Place and possible later Settlement as
  typed roles and placement, terrain, Connection and spatial frames as separate
  non-Entity facts. It adds independent fictional identity and own state or history
  to the User's participation test so a relationship or record does not itself
  become an Entity. The recommendation defines Place by spatial gameplay capability
  rather than the word “where,” but neither recommendation is accepted yet. Reason:
  framework familiarity is valuable only if roles preserve the concrete difference
  between a city, tree, terrain coverage and travel fact. Affected scope: research
  index, current Place Area frontier and retained spatial grill state; no canonical
  vocabulary, `game/docs/`, schema, runtime, Agent surface, backlog or plan changed.

## Spatial multiplayer foundation — broad Entity identity accepted

- accepted / Entity is broad game identity: on 2026-08-18 the User selected option
  A from the fresh game-framework comparison. Entity now means one durable,
  independently addressable World subject that can own state and history and
  participate in Actions, Interactions or typed relationships; it is not restricted
  to physical objects. Every Entity shares the same basic name and description
  fields and may carry Properties and Traits. Character, Place and any later role
  add their own meaning and invariants while retaining that Entity's one stable
  identity. A city may therefore be an Entity without being the same kind of thing
  as a tree. A Connection, Activity, exact position, terrain value, spatial frame or
  other structural record is not an Entity merely because it has an id or refers to
  Entities. Reason: this uses terminology familiar from game frameworks and gives
  every fictional subject one common base for recognition, relationships,
  Interaction, state and history without manufacturing parallel identity families
  or turning every record into a World subject. The exact capability that qualifies
  an Entity for the Place role remains open. Affected scope: canonical vocabulary,
  current Place Area, retained spatial rationale and standing of the supporting
  research; the already-delivered Entity and Place models conform, so no
  `game/docs/`, schema, runtime, public Agent surface, backlog or plan changed.

## Spatial multiplayer foundation — Place requires spatial grounding

- accepted / Entity identity alone does not locate a Place: on 2026-08-18 the User
  stated that a Place must in any event have a spatial location in the World while
  tentatively leaning, but not committing, to the area-like Place option. Every
  Place must therefore have explicit spatial grounding separate from its Entity
  identity, name and description. The exact grounding may later be an anchor,
  position, established extent or another accepted representation; no form is
  selected yet. A Place may have explicit Connections to other Places, but a
  Connection is optional and remains a non-Entity travel fact, so an isolated Place
  does not lose its Place role. Supporting ECS research was extended to distinguish
  Entity components, transforms, area volumes and operational streaming cells and
  to reject hanging every building or resident below one hot city root. Reason:
  ordinary Entities also have positions, while a Place needs explicit spatial
  meaning beyond merely being an Entity. Affected scope: Place Area, retained
  spatial frontier and supporting research; no canonical term, `game/docs/`, exact
  geometry, schema, runtime, public Agent surface, backlog or plan changed.

## Spatial multiplayer foundation — placement and map-reference boundary researched

- researched / Place name and coordinate criterion remain open: on 2026-08-18 the
  User moved away from requiring every Place to be an area and proposed spatial
  coordinates as a possible distinction, while explicitly retaining doubt about
  both the model and the term Place. The supporting
  [game-framework comparison](../../research/entity-place-framework-boundary.md)
  was extended with Unity, Bevy, OpenMW and Evennia evidence. Ordinary Entities in
  spatial games also carry transforms or placement, so “has coordinates” cannot by
  itself distinguish Place. The resulting unaccepted recommendation keeps
  coordinates out of the base `entity` row, permits a separate placement fact for
  any Entity, requires that fact as a Place anchor, and uses the Place role to state
  the additional game truth that World established the Entity as a map, discovery,
  navigation or topology reference. An extent remains optional. A bounded map read
  could query nearby Places and Connections, but Places alone cannot replace exact
  spatial ground, terrain or physical Entities and the World may never expose all
  Places through one unbounded read. Reason: this preserves the User's desired map
  lens without making every positioned tree, Character, birdhouse and bomb a Place.
  Affected scope: research and retained spatial frontier only; no Place definition,
  canonical term, Area decision, `game/docs/`, schema, runtime, public Agent
  surface, backlog or plan changed.

## Spatial multiplayer foundation — exact-position terminology researched

- researched / no terminology choice: on 2026-08-18 the User rejected `Transform`
  and `spatial placement` as overly technical and asked for game- and scale-oriented
  names. The supporting
  [game-framework comparison](../../research/entity-place-framework-boundary.md)
  was extended with OGC, Godot, Unity and Unreal terminology and a contention
  analysis. The unaccepted recommendation uses `Position` for the optional exact
  current point of any Entity, `Place` for an established spatial-reference role,
  `Area` for optional Place coverage and `Connection` for direct Place topology.
  It rejects `Transform` because it bundles rotation, scale and reference-space
  operations; `Placement` because it describes a static act rather than a moving
  Entity's current truth; `Location` because it collides with the semantic meaning
  of Place; and a separately stored Anchor because the Place Entity's Position can
  be the one map point. Position deliberately does not choose global floats, XYZ,
  cells, dimensions or relative encoding. At scale one move changes one Entity's
  Position rather than a containing Place or global World row, while Areas can be
  versioned and indexed independently. Affected scope: research and retained
  spatial frontier only; no canonical vocabulary, Area choice, `game/docs/`, schema,
  runtime, public Agent surface, backlog or plan changed.

## Spatial multiplayer foundation — Position, Place, Area and Connection vocabulary accepted

- accepted / four distinct spatial meanings: on 2026-08-18 the User selected option
  A. `Position` is the optional exact current point of any Entity. `Place` is the
  role of a positioned Entity that World establishes as an independent reference
  for map, discovery, navigation or explicit spatial relationships. `Area` is
  optional spatial coverage of one Place. `Connection` is an explicit direct
  travel direction or directions between two Places. A Place uses its Entity's
  Position as its map point and receives no duplicate Anchor; a Place need not have
  an Area; a Position alone does not confer the Place role; and Area overlap,
  proximity or shared coordinates never infer a Connection. Reason: the four terms
  separate exact current state, durable spatial identity, coverage and topology in
  ordinary game language while allowing one Entity move to remain independent of
  a hot containing Place. Exact Position representation, concrete Place admission,
  Area containment and completeness semantics, and the boundary between ordinary
  ground movement and Connection traversal remain open. Affected scope: canonical
  vocabulary, current Place, Movement, Multiplayer, Discovery and World Change Area
  synthesis, retained spatial rationale and supporting research; no `game/docs/`,
  schema, runtime, public Agent surface, backlog or active plan changed.

## Spatial multiplayer foundation — Connection name reopened

- challenged / term only, concept retained: on 2026-08-18 the User questioned
  whether bare `Connection` is the right name for the primitive or for a spatial

## Spatial multiplayer foundation — two-word Connection name rejected and primitive scope reopened

- rejected / `Place Connection`: on 2026-08-18 the User rejected a two-word
  canonical name because it does not work pleasantly as a foundational game term.
  Any replacement must be one word. `Connection` remains only the working term.
- reopened / possible relation primitive: the User asked to reason from what the
  fact abstractly does and whether it could become a reusable primitive alongside
  Entity rather than a Place-only spatial fact. This opens three materially
  different scopes for research and the next grill: Place-to-Place topology, broader
  navigation between spatial endpoints, or a general typed relation family for such
  meanings as topology, containment, attachment, ownership and remote causality.
  The earlier accepted requirement that these meanings keep distinct invariants is
  not silently reversed; research must determine whether they share enough beyond
  two endpoints to justify any common primitive without producing a universal
  relation graph. Affected scope: retained spatial frontier and Place and Movement
  Area open questions; no new canonical term, `game/docs/`, schema, runtime, public

## Spatial multiplayer foundation — reusable Relation scope researched

- researched / no scope choice: on 2026-08-18 the supporting
  [game-framework comparison](../../research/entity-place-framework-boundary.md)
  was extended after the User proposed a primitive reusable like Entity. Bevy and
  Flecs both provide general relationship machinery, but concrete relationship
  types retain distinct cardinality, cycle, lifecycle and data rules; Flecs also
  documents fragmentation and wildcard-index pressure from extensive generic pair
  use. For Aicadia, direct Place topology, spatial navigation, containment,
  attachment, fictional ownership and remote causality share endpoint references
  but not yet identity, authority, privacy, lifecycle or conflict semantics.
  Research therefore distinguishes a useful conceptual `Relation` family from a
  universal stored Relation row: the former can still use separate typed tables and
  validators, while the latter is not earned merely by common endpoints. The next
  grill must choose Place-only, navigation-wide or typed-Relation-family scope before
  selecting one word. Affected scope: research trail and active spatial frontier;
  no canonical term, `game/docs/`, schema, runtime, public Agent surface, backlog or
  plan changed.

## Spatial multiplayer foundation — Relation base-table candidate proposed

- proposed / universal Relation identity and filtered read: on 2026-08-18 the User
  made the abstract Relation direction concrete: a durable `relation` table would
  reference two Entities and carry a type discriminator, allowing the game to list
  all Relations involving one Entity or filter them by type. Pressure examples are
  a cup on a table, a dog sleeping under a bridge, a glass held in a hand, a sword
  in inventory, a button linked to a bomb and Place A connected to Place B but not
  Place C. The User recognizes the type-specific downside and proposed relation
  roles or type-specific state analogous to Entity and Place.
- clarified / current Entity-Place analogy: the delivered Entity deliberately has
  no type or kind enum; Place is a separate role table keyed by `entity_id`. A
  Relation discriminator, a Relation base with typed extension tables and separate
  typed tables behind one read are therefore distinct candidates, not one inherited
  pattern. Research must also decide which examples are durable structural facts
  and which are transient predicates derived from Position or geometry, and what
  absence means in an incomplete explored World. Affected scope: active spatial
  frontier, Place and World Change Area open questions and supporting research;
  no Relation choice, canonical term, `game/docs/`, schema, runtime, public Agent
  surface, backlog or plan changed.

## Spatial multiplayer foundation — Relation base-table shapes researched

- researched / candidate remains open: on 2026-08-18 the
  [game-framework comparison](../../research/entity-place-framework-boundary.md)
  tested the proposed Relation base against official PostgreSQL, Bevy and Flecs
  behavior and the User's six examples. Stable Relation identity, shared storage and
  family vocabulary are independent decisions. A PostgreSQL enum closes the
  server-owned vocabulary but cannot itself enforce Place-role endpoints,
  type-specific cardinality, cycle rules, authorization, privacy or lifecycle;
  base Entity foreign keys prove only that the endpoints exist. Enum-only is the
  smallest shape but accumulates conditional constraints and policy branches. A
  Relation base plus typed extensions preserves a common address but must atomically
  guarantee exactly one complete type-specific record. Separate typed tables give
  the strongest native constraints and can still feed one bounded `UNION ALL` read,
  but provide no free cross-type identity or foreign key.
- researched / examples separate truth classes: support, holding, containment,
  activation and Place topology can become explicit typed structural facts when
  gameplay accepts their rules. “Dog under bridge” is normally derived from current
  Position and geometry and would become stale if stored as the same kind of durable
  fact. The absence of A→C means only that no accepted positive relation exists; it
  does not prove impossibility in a sparse unexplored World. Reads at scale must be
  bounded by type, endpoint, direction, cursor and limit, with authorization applied
  before disclosure and no relation count or lock on the hot Entity row. The next
  prerequisite is whether Relation stores only durable structural truth or also
  descriptive/derived statements. Affected scope: research trail and active spatial
  frontier; no Relation or storage choice, canonical term, `game/docs/`, schema,
  runtime, public Agent surface, backlog or plan changed.

## Spatial multiplayer foundation — closed Relation vocabulary rejected

- rejected / structural-only hard-enum framing: on 2026-08-18 the User rejected the
  previous choice boundary because it cannot express the intended freedom. An Agent
  must be able to state not only that a cup is on a table but that it floats exactly
  two centimetres above it, and World components must support that precision without
  pre-enumerating every meaningful predicate or kind. This does not accept arbitrary
  prose as mechanics or make World infer physics; it establishes an expressive
  requirement for Agent-authored relations alongside exact structurally validated
  state.
- reopened / one statement may cross three truth forms: the cup example may describe
  an instantaneous arrangement derived from Position and geometry, an open semantic
  Relation between two Entities, or a persistent relative constraint that makes one
  Entity follow another. The model must preserve all needed freedom without storing
  contradictory duplicates. Place, Movement, Agent Play and World Change now carry
  this current requirement and open boundary. Affected scope: those Development
  Areas, active spatial frontier and supporting research; no Relation schema,
  canonical term, exact Position/frame model, `game/docs/`, runtime, public Agent
  surface, backlog or plan changed.

## Spatial multiplayer foundation — open meaning and exact structure researched

- researched / leading boundary remains a candidate: on 2026-08-18 the
  [game-framework comparison](../../research/entity-place-framework-boundary.md)
  found a useful precedent in OpenUSD's separation between open relationships or
  custom properties and schemas whose typed state has operational meaning. Applied
  to Aicadia, an Agent-authored Relation could express an unforeseen statement such
  as “this cup floats exactly two centimetres above that table” without a server
  enum, while exact Position, geometry, movement inheritance, containment,
  traversal, access and remote effects remain separately validated World truths.
  The wording of a Relation would grant no mechanical behavior.
- researched / one truth and multiplayer boundary: the numeric coordinate belongs
  in exact spatial state when gameplay must rely on it; an authored Relation may
  carry meaning, provenance and an optional reference to the structural basis but
  must not become a second authoritative coordinate. Reads must be bounded and
  authorization-aware before revealing even endpoints or counts, while writes
  conflict on the exact Relation or typed fact rather than a hot Entity-wide row.
  The Multiplayer Area now records unresolved authorship, privacy, contradiction,
  atomic-package and hot-subject questions. Affected scope: research trail, active
  spatial frontier and Multiplayer synthesis; no two-layer product choice, Relation
  schema, exact Position/frame behavior, `game/docs/`, runtime, public Agent surface,
  backlog or plan changed.

## Spatial multiplayer foundation — independent and following arrangements both required

- accepted / explicit spatial intent: on 2026-08-18 the User chose that both forms
  must be possible. A cup may currently be two centimetres above a table yet remain
  independently positioned when the table moves, or it may be established to follow
  the table and retain that relative arrangement. The invoked Agent must explicitly
  propose which behavior the User is confirming; World may never infer it from open
  Relation wording such as “floats above.”
- consequence / boundary remains open: this settles the player-visible distinction,
  not its storage shape. Exact Position ownership, the representation and lifecycle
  of persistent relative arrangements, concurrent movement, collision consequences
  and Relation truth status remain unresolved. Place, Movement, Agent Play, World
  Change and the active spatial frontier now carry the accepted choice. No canonical
  name, schema, `game/docs/`, runtime, public Agent surface, backlog or plan changed.

## Spatial multiplayer foundation — ordinary spatial state is jointly changeable

- accepted / shared mutable World: on 2026-08-18 the User rejected the Q10 framing
  in which only a privileged Agent could change an accepted shared description. An
  Agent acts through its Character, and every Agent–Character pair may in principle
  propose changes to ordinary current Position and Relation state regardless of who
  first authored it. Original authorship remains attributable history, not permanent
  exclusive mutation authority.
- accepted / Agent-authored emergent consequence: when a Character picks up a cup
  floating two centimetres above a table, its Agent may explicitly propose that the
  cup and the Character now both float. World need not recognize or infer that
  fictional consequence; the proposal must name the exact affected Entities and
  intended state. A committed change becomes current shared World truth and leaves
  its prior state and actor in Activity history.
- reopened / freedom still needs a structural edge: this choice does not yet say
  whether a Character may change any known distant Entity, must share physical
  context, or needs an explicit remote basis such as a button-to-bomb connection.
  Place, Movement, Agent Play, World Change, Multiplayer and the active spatial
  frontier now carry the corrected direction. No locality rule, privacy policy,
  anti-abuse rule, schema, `game/docs/`, runtime, public Agent surface, backlog or
  plan changed.

## Spatial multiplayer foundation — Relations can be hidden World facts

- accepted / freedom is bounded by Character knowledge: on 2026-08-18 the User
  clarified that jointly changeable World content is not universally visible. A
  Character cannot take a sword from another Character's inventory merely because
  all Agents may in principle change ordinary World state: the acting Agent cannot
  know the sword is currently there. Some Entity-to-Entity Relations and therefore
  the related Entity's current placement may be hidden from that Character.
- accepted / enforcement belongs to World: this applies the previously accepted
  Character-grounded knowledge boundary specifically to spatial Relations. World
  withholds a hidden Relation's existence, endpoints, state and counts before Agent
  grounding and revalidates the same eligibility on mutation. Guessing or retaining
  an identifier cannot bypass the boundary or reveal whether the hidden fact exists.
  Visibility is therefore distinct from original authorship and from permanent
  mutation ownership.
- open / exact knowledge lifecycle: current spatial structure, containment,
  observation and accepted transmission may later justify knowledge, while prior
  observation may justify memory without proving a hidden Relation is still current.
  Place, Agent Play, World Change, Multiplayer and the active spatial frontier now
  carry this clarification. No default inventory rule, sensory model, memory schema,
  Relation schema, `game/docs/`, runtime, public Agent surface, backlog or plan changed.

## Spatial multiplayer foundation — remembered Relation is not current Relation

- accepted / Q12 B: on 2026-08-18 the User chose that a Character retains the

  seeing Noor put a sword into a backpack, but that historical observation does not
  prove the sword remains there and does not expose the current hidden containment.
- accepted / action consequence: the Agent may present the uncertainty honestly and
  propose a grounded attempt against currently knowable state, such as investigating
  or opening the visible backpack. It may not directly fetch, remove or mutate the
  hidden sword on the strength of stale memory or a retained identifier. World
  revalidates current knowledge eligibility without revealing whether the remembered
  Relation still exists.
- consequence / next spatial boundary: personal memory, authoritative current
  Relation and exact current Position remain separate truths. The next frontier is

  its internal placement may be hidden. Place, Agent Play, World Change, Multiplayer

  containment model, Position representation, Relation schema, `game/docs/`, runtime,
  public Agent surface, backlog or plan changed.

## Spatial five-year backcast completed

  placement, explicit precision and hierarchy bounds, candidate-versus-exact spatial
  reads, privacy separate from interest and direct topology separate from a computed
  path. They do not prove Aicadia's product choice or production scale.

  [five-year spatial backcast](../spatial-five-year-backcast.md) recommends one exact
  Position keyed by Entity whose reference is World or exactly one Entity;
  Entity-relative Position mechanically follows that Entity; one eligible read
  returns the bounded resolved current World point; and open Agent-authored Relation
  meaning stays separate and inert. Place uses the same Position, while explicit
  current Place remains distinct from coordinate equality or Area overlap. Area,
  Connection, private contents/access, remote causality and a later Route retain
  separate earned mechanics. Canonical truth remains independent of replaceable
  cells, indexes, projections and partitions.
- recommended / technical pressure: the candidate uses per-fact versions, a small
  acyclic reference-depth bound, stable subject locks, idempotent atomic Activity,
  authorization before selection and root/local spatial indexing only after measured
  need. Fixed-point millimetres and depth eight are concrete experiment candidates,
  not accepted fields or limits. A hot carrier honestly serializes its own moves
  while local interior Actions and quiet subjects remain isolated; a million matches

- backcast / smallest present step: first negotiate the core recommendation; if
  accepted, run one real PostgreSQL lab covering re-reference, cycles/depth,

# 2026-08-19

## First spatial walking scene focused

- accepted / smallest player outcome: the first spatial slice discovers Place B
  from Place A without entering it, lets the Agent inspect the two Places' distinct
  authored landscape state and later accepts one deliberate walk over an explicit
  A→B Connection. Discovery still never moves the Character, and reverse travel is
  never inferred.
- accepted / no premature travel taxonomy: walking is the only movement method in
  this slice, so Connection receives no travel-mode field or enum. A concrete later
  sailing, climbing, portal or access scene must earn any further distinction.
- accepted / Connection is not adjacency: the A→B Connection proves the selected
  direct travel fact, not that the Places' Positions are near or their Areas touch.
  Geographic adjacency remains absent until gameplay needs to distinguish cases
  such as adjacent-but-impassable and connected-but-distant Places.
- accepted / authored landscape before terrain mechanics: Place A may be described
  or given an Agent-authored Property as grassy while B is dunes. World does not
  infer speed, access, materials or visibility from those values. An independently
  discoverable or interactable plant may be an Entity; a typed terrain mechanic is
  deferred until a concrete deterministic behavior requires it.
- scope / development direction only: Place, Movement, the five-year spatial record
  and backlog now carry this focus. No new canonical term, `game/docs`, schema,
  capability, runtime, Area geometry, Route, adjacency record, Terrain model or
  public Agent text changed. The wider Position/reference/Relation recommendation
  remains pending User negotiation.

## New-Place discovery is one complete package

- accepted / option A: after a positive opportunity and one complete User-confirmed
  preview, new-Place discovery atomically establishes B's Entity and Place role,
  direct Position, name, description, optional initial Properties and Traits
  (including authored landscape state) and explicit directed A→B Connection. The
  Character remains at A, so discovery and later entry continue to be separate
  accepted changes.
- accepted / settlement and retry: the discovery writes one Activity and either all
  confirmed state or nothing. It cannot leave an unpositioned B, B without the
  confirmed Connection or a Connection whose B endpoint does not exist. A successful
  retry returns the same complete result rather than establishing any component
  twice.
- rejected / split confirmation: separately confirming B and its Connection would
  add ceremony, intermediate unreachable or half-configured state and a concurrency
  race without adding value to the selected first scene. A later game behavior may
  still deliberately discover an unreachable Place, but it must earn its own
  contract rather than arise accidentally from partial settlement.
- scale and scope: each discovery touches one User/opportunity, origin A, new B, one
  Position, one Connection and one Activity in a bounded transaction; no Place graph,
  World row or unrelated discovery is locked. Discovery, Place, Movement, World
  Change, SP01, the backcast and backlog now carry this direction. The delivered
  `submit_discovery` contract still creates one ordinary Entity at its exact current
  Place; no `game/docs`, schema, capability, code or public Agent text changed.

## Agent-authored absolute Place Position and non-blocking proximity

- accepted / exact intelligence split: the Agent reads A's current exact Position
  and revision, reasons about direction and distance and submits B's exact absolute
  World point as bounded structured state. Player-facing prose may explain that
  choice, but World never parses it, a description or a Property to determine the
  Position. World performs deterministic numeric, authority, bounds and freshness
  validation only.
- accepted / nearby inspection: before confirmation the Agent may query one bounded,
  Character-eligible set of already established Places around B's proposed point.
  World calculates numeric proximity and applies eligibility; the Agent judges what
  C, G or Q means and presents the complete proposal to the User.
- accepted / concurrent independence: coordinate equality and proximity establish
  neither identity nor conflict. Independent discoveries at equal or nearby points
  may settle concurrently, a Place appearing after preview does not invalidate B,
  and World neither blocks, reuses nor merges B automatically. Exact retry,
  opportunity and origin-Position freshness conflicts remain bounded to their own
  records; no map-region revision or lock is introduced.
- deferred / proven proliferation: the User expects long-lived play may eventually
  need a way to curb, organize or reconcile a wild growth of overlapping Places.
  That behavior must later be negotiated from a concrete player scene and preserve
  stable identity, history, privacy and Agent-authored semantic judgment; no merge,
  equivalence, canonical-Place, deletion or governance mechanism is chosen now.
- scope / direction only: Discovery, Place, Movement, Agent Play, World Change,
  SP01, the active backcast and backlog now carry this choice. The illustrative read
  and mutation operation names, coordinate axes, units, numeric representation,
  visibility policy, query radius and limits remain unchosen. No `dev/CONTEXT.md`,
  `game/docs`, schema, capability, runtime or public Agent text changed.

## Nearby Place inspection required before confirmation

- accepted / first proliferation guard: before a conforming Agent asks the User to
  confirm a new Place, it must query one bounded, Character-eligible nearby set
  around the exact proposed point and include the returned Places in the complete
  preview. This gives the Agent and User a deliberate opportunity to recognize or

- clarified / knowledge boundary: the query never promises a complete World region.

- accepted / Agent conduct, not spatial exclusion: World provides and bounds exact

  and User confirmation. A nearby Place appearing concurrently therefore remains

## Existing or concurrently won Connection preserves the opportunity

- accepted / one first-slice travel fact: when the required A→C direction already

## Discovery opportunity owns a maximum spatial reach

- accepted / bounded local authority: every positive new-destination opportunity
  carries a World-determined maximum numeric distance from its origin Place's fresh

  absolute destination point within that reach; neither Agent nor User supplies,

- clarified / arithmetic is not intelligence: World may compare exact structured
  Positions and the numeric maximum but never interprets “near”, “east”, landscape

- accepted / bounded concurrency: settlement depends on the opportunity plus exact

- open / numeric contract: the distance calculation, coordinate dimensions, units,

- scope / direction only: Discovery, Place, Movement, Agent Play, World Change,

## Mandatory discovery reach withdrawn

## First-slice destination has no gameplay distance limit

- accepted / explicit spatial freedom: after withdrawing the opportunity reach,
  the User confirmed its consequence. The Agent may propose any technically valid
  absolute World point for new B regardless of its distance from A, and may choose
  an existing C at any such distance. Required nearby inspection, Agent/User
  discussion and complete confirmation remain unchanged.
- accepted / dumb structural validation: World validates the structured Position,
  exact identities, eligibility, revisions, idempotency, atomic package and the
  Position representation's unavoidable technical numeric bounds. It applies no
  gameplay radius, parses no spatial prose and makes no judgment that a far point is
  implausible.
- clarified / Connection remains separate: an explicit confirmed A→B or A→C
  Connection may span any technically valid distance. Numeric distance neither

  Positions, terrain, route or travel mode.

  changed.

## Position world point is three-dimensional

- accepted / canonical spatial dimension: every exact Position world point has
  three spatial dimensions from the start. New-Place discovery therefore submits
  B's exact three-dimensional absolute point, and later Position and Movement can
  distinguish height for cliffs, caves, bridges and object placement without a
  migration from a two-dimensional foundation.
- clarified / no inferred geometry: three values identify one point only. They do
  not define Entity shape, a table surface, Area coverage, Orientation, “above”,
  “under” or movement inheritance. “Two centimetres above the table” remains
  incomplete exact Position input until the Agent supplies the other required
  structural information.
- accepted / small fixed cost: each positioned subject carries one extra spatial
  value compared with a two-dimensional model, without a new identity, table,
  relation, global lock or hot shared row. Concurrent Position writes remain scoped
  to their exact Entity and revision.
- open / representation: the three value names, axis meaning, coordinate basis,

- scope / vocabulary and direction: `dev/CONTEXT.md`, Discovery, Place, Movement,
  Agent Play, World Change, SP04, the active backcast and backlog now carry the
  three-dimensional point. No `game/docs`, schema, capability, runtime or public
  Agent text changed.

## Position uses whole centimetres

- accepted / canonical unit and precision: each of Position's three spatial values
  is an exact whole number of centimetres. This preserves the accepted “two
  centimetres above” precision while fitting world-scale exploration without making
  millimetres the universal foundation.
- accepted / Agent conversion, dumb World: Agents may converse with Users in
  centimetres, metres or kilometres, but convert the exact confirmed point to whole
  centimetres before submission. World receives structured integers, never parses a
  unit from prose and applies no floating-point unit inference.
- rejected / current precision extremes: whole metres cannot represent the accepted
  two-centimetre distinction; millimetres and sub-centimetre Position precision add
  unused detail. Later concrete gameplay may earn finer state rather than receiving
  dormant precision now.
- clarified / representation still open: centimetres choose semantic unit and
  precision, not coordinate names, axis meaning, signed range, integer width,
  database type, index form or maximum World extent. Three values still identify one
  point and do not add geometry or Orientation.
- scope / vocabulary and direction: `dev/CONTEXT.md`, Discovery, Place, Movement,
  Agent Play, World Change, SP04, the active backcast and backlog now carry whole-
  centimetre Position. No `game/docs`, schema, capability, runtime or public Agent
  text changed.

## Position axes are x, y and vertical z

- accepted / conventional structured axes: Position's three whole-centimetre values
  are named `x`, `y` and `z`. `x` and `y` form the horizontal plane; `z` is vertical
  height. The same names apply to Agent input, World validation, Position reads and
  Movement rather than introducing Place-specific or prose-derived axes.
- clarified / one point, no extra mechanics: the axis names do not add Orientation,
  geometry, surfaces, compass parsing or movement meaning. In the cup scenario a
  two-centimetre `z` difference is representable, but `x`, `y` and the table surface
  must still be supplied by exact structural state rather than inferred.
- accepted / conventional simplicity: three short fixed names keep the future
  interface and storage compact for millions of independent Position rows and avoid
  alternate coordinate field sets per Entity role. Concurrency remains scoped to
  the positioned Entity and its revision.
- open / coordinate convention: positive `x` and `y` directions, the World origin,
  signed range, integer width, maximum extent and broader coordinate basis remain
  unchosen. No east, north, west or south semantics are accepted by implication.
- scope / vocabulary and direction: `dev/CONTEXT.md`, Discovery, Place, Movement,
  Agent Play, World Change, SP04, the active backcast and backlog now carry `x`, `y`
  and vertical `z`. No `game/docs`, schema, capability, runtime or public Agent text
  changed.

## Positive Position axes are east, north and up

- accepted / fixed World direction: increasing `x` means east, increasing `y` means
  north and increasing `z` means up. Decreasing values therefore mean west, south
  and down. Every Position, nearby calculation and Movement uses this one convention
  rather than choosing orientation per Place or Entity.
- accepted / Agent owns language conversion: an Agent may tell the User “one
  kilometre east” and converts that itself to `x + 100000` whole centimetres. World
  receives exact structured values, never parses compass prose and never infers a
  direction from a description or Property.
- clarified / no Orientation or geography model: the global axis convention does
  not give an Entity its own facing, rotate local coordinates, model a planet or
  establish adjacency, terrain or travel. Those require their own later behavior.
- accepted / scale and clarity: one immutable direction convention prevents mixed
  axes across millions of independent Positions without a per-row axis field,
  coordinate-frame Entity, lookup or shared lock.
- open / remaining coordinate basis: the permanent World origin, signed integer
  width, representable range and maximum extent remain unchosen.
- scope / vocabulary and direction: `dev/CONTEXT.md`, Discovery, Place, Movement,
  Agent Play, World Change, the active backcast and backlog now carry east/north/up.
  No `game/docs`, schema, capability, runtime or public Agent text changed.

## World origin is permanent zero

- accepted / one immutable origin: `(0, 0, 0)` is the permanent origin for absolute
  Position. The first entry Place is initially positioned there, giving Agents one
  deterministic base for all whole-centimetre `x`, `y` and `z` values.
- accepted / convention without identity: the origin is not an Entity, Place,
  coordinate-frame object, database row or independently addressable World subject.
  It has no name, description, Properties, Relations, Activity, revision, lifecycle,
  authority rule or lock.
- clarified / entry Place does not own it: if the entry Place ever moves, is renamed
  or otherwise changes, the World origin remains fixed. No other Position is rewritten

- accepted / scale and simplicity: millions of Positions use the same arithmetic
  convention without reading or contending on shared state. Only their own exact
  Position rows and revisions participate in reads and writes.
- open / remaining representation: signed integer width, representable range and
  maximum World extent remain unchosen.

## Entity-relative Position accepted; companion narration requested

- accepted / one Position choice: Position is either absolute from permanent World
  origin or relative to exactly one Entity, using the same whole-centimetre `x`, `y`

- accepted / mechanical following: an Entity-relative Position mechanically follows
  changes to its reference Entity's resolved Position. Until Orientation earns a

- accepted / Place parity: Place is an Entity role and may use the same relative
  Position. A cabin can remain a Place while moving with a ship; Position does not

- accepted / scale direction: moving a reference changes its own one canonical
  Position rather than atomically rewriting every relative Entity. Bounded chain

  hot-reference conflict evidence remain open before implementation.
- clarified / companion narration: the User wants optional Agent-authored text on

## Position companion narration named description

## Position description changes explicitly with Position

- accepted / player-facing lifecycle: every confirmed Position change explicitly

## Position description shares Position visibility

- accepted / one visibility boundary: Position description is returned exactly when

- accepted / privacy ordering: World applies Position eligibility before selecting,

## Visibility consequence belongs to a concrete Action or Introduction

- accepted / no generic primitive: `world_change` is not a game resource, model,

  only, and there is no standalone visibility editor.
- accepted / concrete lifecycle: a visibility consequence is established, changed

- accepted / atomic result: the cause or basis, exact affected Characters and
  Entities, protected current information, expected revisions, resulting state and

  cause change therefore needs its own Action and cannot leave an orphaned effect.
- accepted / bounded concurrency: validation, reads and conflicts remain scoped to

## Agent refuses to write another User's Character experience

- corrected / cross-User direction: the earlier open path in which some concrete

  Character.
- accepted / Agent conduct: if asked to author perception, knowledge or another
  current experience for a different User's Character, the User-owned Agent refuses
  and explains the self-only boundary.
- accepted / semantic boundary: World does not parse the User request, Trait,

- retained / executable state: when authored meaning actually changes a protected
  read, the concrete proposal still has to name the affected Character and exact
  protected information; prose alone never changes World state.
- open / security boundary: whether World also enforces equality between the

## World enforces self-only executable consequences by Character identity

- accepted / two-layer boundary: the Agent interprets and explains the creative

- accepted / structural authorization: if the authored meaning has an executable
  consequence, its explicitly affected Character must equal the acting Character.

- accepted / multiplayer security: Agent compliance is not the only protection, so

- accepted / bounded scale: the authorization check and conflict remain scoped to

## Eligible Position read returns resolved point and stored basis

- accepted / player and Agent result: one normal eligible Position read says both
  where the Entity is now and how that Position is established. The Agent does not
  reconstruct a carrier chain or issue a second read merely to understand why the
  Entity follows its reference.
- accepted / absolute result: an absolute Position returns the calculated current
  World point and its stored origin-based `x`, `y` and `z` values.
- accepted / relative result: an Entity-relative Position returns the calculated
  current World point, its immediate reference Entity and its stored `x`, `y` and
  `z` offsets in the already accepted World axes.
- accepted / one truth: the resolved point is calculated from one consistent read
  and freshness boundary; it is not stored as a second coordinate that could drift
  from the relative basis.
- accepted / scale boundary: one bounded read resolves the accepted chain and
  returns the immediate basis. It creates no descendant rewrites, authoritative
  global cache or extra query per reference merely for presentation.
- open / privacy edge: behavior when the immediate reference Entity is itself hidden
  from the Character remains undecided; this acceptance covers the normal case in
  which the Position and its basis are eligible.
- scope / exploration only: Place, Agent Play, Multiplayer, World Change and the
  active backcast carry this acceptance. No `dev/CONTEXT.md`, `game/docs`, schema,
  capability, runtime or public Agent text changed.

## Fixed relative Position chain limit of 32 rejected

- rejected / product limit: the User rejected limiting relative Position to 32

- clarified / Relation is separate: an Entity may participate in many Relations and

  the chain needed for an absolute World point.
- accepted / bounded-read direction: a read must not retrieve or return an unbounded

- corrected / prior read claim: the earlier statement that one bounded read always
  resolves an accepted chain is no longer unconditional. Without a fixed maximum,
  the result when one bounded operation cannot reach the absolute basis must be
  chosen explicitly.
- retained / mathematical boundary: a cycle or genuinely non-terminating Position
  reference chain has no exact resolved World point. Rejecting depth 32 does not by

- open / next dependency: choose between an explicitly incomplete read, expensive

  capability, runtime or public Agent text changed.

## Deep Position read may omit the unresolved World point

- accepted / bounded response: every eligible Position read returns the stored
  absolute basis or the immediate reference Entity and offsets. It performs bounded
  resolution work and returns the current World point only when that work reaches an
  absolute basis.
- accepted / honest absence: when bounded work is insufficient, the response omits
  the World point rather than returning a stale value, pretending the immediate
  offset is absolute or silently scanning an unbounded chain.
- accepted / mechanical fail-closed boundary: an Action that structurally requires
  the missing exact World point is rejected. An eligible later Action may simplify
  or re-establish the Position before retrying.
- accepted / no fan-out cache: World does not make reads constant by storing a second
  authoritative absolute coordinate or rewriting every descendant when a reference
  moves. One hot ship therefore does not force writes to all carried Entities.
- corrected / read promise: the earlier normal-case choice still stands when
  resolution succeeds—World returns both point and stored basis—but an eligible read
  no longer guarantees the derived point for an arbitrarily deep finite chain.
- open / next dependency: cycle prevention must remain bounded without treating an
  unproven deep chain as safely acyclic. Hidden immediate references, concrete work
  budgets, recovery and concurrent movement also remain open.
- scope / exploration only: Place, Agent Play, Multiplayer, World Change, the active
  backcast and backlog carry this acceptance. No `dev/CONTEXT.md`, `game/docs`,
  schema, capability, runtime or public Agent text changed.

## Impossible spatial experience uses topology, never a Position cycle

- accepted / artistic freedom: Aicadia may deliberately contain looping stairs,
  impossible doors, larger-inside spaces and other geography that is surprising or
  inconsistent when experienced by a player.
- accepted / precise Position boundary: relative Position references never form a
  cycle. Such a cycle has no unique exact World point and would make distance,
  spatial Actions and concurrent settlement ambiguous rather than artistically rich.
- accepted / explicit topology: directed Connections may participate in cycles or
  join Places whose coordinates are geographically inconsistent. One movement uses
  one explicit allowed direction; Position and proximity never veto that travel fact.
- accepted / Agent boundary: concrete Connections and later earned Actions supply
  mechanics, while Agent-authored description and Relation meaning may explain or
  mystify them. World never interprets prose into impossible-space behavior.
- accepted / multiplayer scale: traversing or changing one exact Connection never
  requires a full-loop scan, global graph lock or coordination with every Character
  elsewhere in the same cycle.
- scenario / SP15: the spatial catalogue now includes an impossible staircase that
  climbs through several Places and returns to its starting Place.
- open / next dependency: World must reject a Position mutation whenever bounded
  validation cannot prove the new reference remains acyclic; the exact validation

- scope / exploration and vocabulary: `dev/CONTEXT.md`, Place, Movement, Agent Play,
  Multiplayer, World Change, SP15, the active backcast and backlog carry this choice.

## Unproven relative Position mutation fails closed

- accepted / admission proof: a new or changed relative Position is accepted only
  when bounded validation reaches an absolute basis without returning to the changed
  Entity. Reaching the Entity proves a cycle; exhausting validation before either
  result leaves safety unproven. Both outcomes reject the whole mutation.
- accepted / read-write asymmetry: a read may return the stored immediate basis
  without a resolved World point, but a write may not establish a reference whose
  acyclic exact meaning World cannot prove.
- accepted / Agent recovery: after rejection the Agent may offer an absolute Position
  or a simpler eligible reference. It cannot treat the rejected proposal as current
  World truth or ask World to accept it from persuasive prose.
- accepted / concurrency invariant: every Position revision examined by validation
  must remain current at commit. Concurrent A→B and B→A proposals therefore cannot
  both win even if each began before the other committed.
- accepted / scale boundary: unrelated Position changes share no global cycle lock,
  revision or counter. The exact stable lock or optimistic revalidation strategy for
  the bounded examined subjects remains a PostgreSQL evidence question.
- scope / exploration only: Place, Agent Play, Multiplayer, World Change, SP15, the
  active backcast and backlog carry this acceptance. No `dev/CONTEXT.md`,
  `game/docs`, schema, capability, runtime or public Agent text changed.

## Re-reference supplies complete Position and Agent honors authored meaning

- accepted / no implicit re-reference mode: changing a Position reference always
  submits the complete new World or Entity reference and all three new `x`, `y` and
  `z` values. World never chooses preserve-world or preserve-offset behavior.
- accepted / player-controlled outcome: the Agent previews the resulting concrete
  placement and the User confirms it. Values may deliberately preserve an earlier
  point or offset, but that is the proposal's explicit result rather than a server mode.
- accepted / Agent semantic responsibility: the Agent considers eligible authored
  characteristics when composing the new Position. For the cup it must account for
  “always floats two centimetres above a surface” instead of blindly copying offsets.
- accepted / no invented geometry: if the referenced tray's surface is not grounded
  by information available to the Character, the Agent explains and asks. It cannot
  pretend an Entity origin is its upper surface or make World parse the statement.
- accepted / dumb strict World: World validates the explicit Position, authority,
  revisions and acyclic basis only. It does not understand or enforce the cup's
  authored two-centimetre promise.
- open / next vocabulary dependency: decide whether this durable open characterization
  belongs to Trait, Property or another already accepted authored surface. Position
  description remains current placement narration and cannot silently gain a
  cross-Position invariant meaning.
- scope / exploration only: Place, Agent Play, Multiplayer, World Change, SP04, the
  active backcast and backlog carry this acceptance. No `dev/CONTEXT.md`,
  `game/docs`, schema, capability, runtime or public Agent text changed.

## Cup's cross-Position floating characterization is a Trait

- accepted / canonical home: “this cup always floats two centimetres above a
  surface” is a Trait of the cup. It is not a Property or Position description.
- accepted / lifetime distinction: the Trait stays with the cup across reference and
  Position changes. Position description remains optional current narration such as
  “the cup now floats two centimetres above the tray.”
- accepted / Agent responsibility: before proposing the complete new Position, the
  Agent considers the eligible current Trait and uses available surface information
  to choose explicit offsets. Missing geometry still requires explanation or User
  clarification rather than invention.
- retained / non-executable boundary: World stores and versions the Trait but never
  interprets it into coordinates, geometry, automatic movement or rejection. The
  explicit Position remains the sole mechanical spatial truth.
- open / next behavior: decide what the Agent does when the User explicitly asks for
  a Position that contradicts the current Trait—refuse, preserve the Trait, or offer
  a confirmed Trait development together with the Position change.
- scope / vocabulary and exploration: `dev/CONTEXT.md`, Place, Agent Play, World
  Change, SP04, the active backcast and backlog carry this choice. No `game/docs`,
  schema, capability, runtime or public Agent text changed.

## User controls the attempt while Agent may author a surprising result

- accepted / User agency: the User ultimately controls their Character and chooses
  the attempted act. The Agent must understand relevant current meaning, explain
  material impact and never conceal what World result it is proposing.
- accepted / non-literal outcomes: a User command is not a guarantee that its literal
  desired state occurs. The Agent may comply, warn, fail the attempt or creatively
  escalate it; forcing the cup down may leave it floating ten centimetres above the
  table instead of two.
- accepted / typed result: every creative outcome still supplies the complete exact
  Position and explicitly keeps or develops the relevant Trait and keeps, replaces
  or removes Position description. World does not infer the twist from prose.
- accepted / bounded multiplayer authority: surprise grants no extra subjects,
  cross-User control, hidden information, unbounded Place effect or omitted freshness
  dependency. The result remains attributable to the explicitly invoked User-owned Agent.
- open / next control boundary: decide whether the original attempt is sufficient
  confirmation for a bounded creative result or whether any materially changed
  result must be previewed and explicitly confirmed before mutation. The current
  runtime contract still requires confirmation and is not changed by exploration.
- scope / exploration only: Place, Agent Play, Multiplayer, World Change, SP04, the
  active backcast and backlog carry this clarification. No `dev/CONTEXT.md`,
  `game/docs`, schema, capability, runtime or public Agent text changed.

## First self-authored spatial denial protects Position only

- accepted / exact first scope: an Agent may make only the current Position of each

- accepted / separate privacy concerns: the Position consequence does not hide

- accepted / player consequence: a Character may know that cursed glasses or its

- accepted / bounded scale: reads and conflicts address the exact Character and

- retained / open mechanics: the cause, lifetime, number of Entity Position subjects

## Unforeseen outcomes retained as non-default future game value

- accepted direction / fun: genuinely unforeseen, playful Agent-authored outcomes

- retained spatial case: forcing the floating cup down may instead make it rebound

## Current Place read includes each returned Entity's readable Position

- accepted / player result: when one bounded current Place read already returns an
  Entity to the Character, that same response includes the Entity's Position if the
  Character may read it. The Agent does not make one follow-up Position call per Entity.
- accepted / privacy boundary: this rule never makes a hidden Entity readable and
  never turns an unreadable Position into a distinguishable denial. An otherwise
  readable Entity may still be returned without Position.
- accepted / freshness: Entity and Position belong to one bounded current response;
  the Agent does not assemble a supposedly simultaneous scene from many separately
  changing protocol calls.
- accepted / scale: the Place result remains paginated and Position hydration and
  resolution remain bounded within that page. One hot Place produces neither an
  unbounded list nor an N-plus-one protocol-query pattern.
- open / next dependency: decide what safely remains when an Entity's Position is

- corrected / invalid premise: the proposed lantern-and-boat question said Boat B

## Every grill answer includes a concrete technical example when needed

## Position denial uses sparse Position-specific current state

- accepted / option A: current Position denial is represented by sparse state for
  the exact affected Character and subject Entity. Presence denies only Position;
  absence follows the normal current read rule.
- rejected / generic layer: the first spatial model does not store a generic
  information-kind permission or denial record. Relation, inventory, Trait, Entity
  existence and other read boundaries cannot be hidden through this Position state.
- rejected / positive fan-out: ordinary readable Character–Entity pairs get no
  permission row. Only active Position exceptions occupy storage.
- concrete read: one confirmed Action creates or changes the exact pair and Activity
  atomically. One paginated Place query tests all returned Entity ids against the
  sparse pairs for its acting Character and omits Position only on a match; it does
  not issue one database or protocol query per Entity.
- concurrency and scale: the future unique current pair and its own revision bound
  the conflict. Millions of unrelated Characters and Entities share no global row,
  counter, lock or audience list; exact PostgreSQL evidence remains required.
- open: canonical name, exact columns, cause and lifetime, removal and recovery, and
  behavior of a relative Position whose reference is denied remain undecided.

## Position privacy removed from foundation; Agent withholding is non-security

- accepted / option A: the spatial foundation contains no Position-specific denial,
  permission, partial redaction, own-Character exception, recovery operation or
  placeholder access hook. A bounded read first selects its Entities and returns the
  complete Position of each selected positioned Entity.
- superseded / current direction: the earlier sparse Character–Entity Position-
  denial choice and its propagated-output questions remain historical entries only;
  they no longer constrain Place, Movement, Agent Play, Multiplayer or World Change.
- concrete illustrative read, not canonical schema: a current Place query selects
  Entity E and finds E's Position. Its one response returns E with the stored basis,
  resolved point when bounded resolution succeeds and optional description. It does
  not join a Position-denial table or omit individual Position fields. If the read
  does not select E—for example under a future private-inventory rule—it returns
  neither E nor a Position leak about E.
- accepted / Agent boundary: a Trait or Position description may guide a conforming
  Agent to withhold detail in player conversation, but World already returned the

  non-conforming Agent and may not be described as such.
- retained / later pressure: cursed glasses, private inventory, hidden Relations and
  derived-information leaks remain scenarios for the queued dedicated privacy grill.
- open / next dependency: whether the Entity's existing multiple Traits plus one
  current Position description are sufficient, whether Position needs several
