## One Position description; Traits remain Entity-owned

- accepted / option A: one current Position has at most one optional description.
  It may contain multiple sentences or paragraphs within ordinary text bounds but
  has no independent identity, ordering, pagination, revision or lifecycle.
- accepted / write behavior: every Position change explicitly keeps, replaces or
  removes that one description against the same Position revision and in the same
  transaction. Two stale competing descriptions therefore cannot both become current.
- accepted / ownership: Position owns no Traits. An Entity may have multiple Traits,
  and durable characteristics stay with that Entity across Position changes.
- concrete illustrative state, not canonical schema: Cup C has Trait T1 “always
  floats two centimetres above a surface” and one Position relative to Table B whose
  description says it currently floats ten centimetres above the oak tabletop. A
  move changes C's Position and explicitly handles its description while T1 remains;
  no Position-Trait or description-child record is created.
- rejected / option B: a separately growing collection of Position descriptions
  would require identities, authorship, ordering, lifecycle, pagination and stale-
  statement reconciliation without a current scenario that needs those costs.
- rejected / option C: making Position a Trait owner would make current placement a
  quasi-subject and leave whether its Traits follow, disappear or orphan on movement
  ambiguous. Cross-Position meaning already has the Entity as its clear owner.
- next dependency: decide whether Entity-relative offsets stay relative only to the
  Entity's one Position point or whether current gameplay requires exact reference
  to a surface or other sub-Entity point. No new concept or canonical name is implied.
- scope / exploration: vocabulary, Place, Agent Play, World Change, the active
  backcast and backlog carry the choice. No `game/docs`, schema, capability, runtime
  or public Agent text changed.

## Relative Position uses only the reference Entity's Position point

- accepted / option A: Entity-relative offsets start at the reference Entity's one
  Position point. The spatial foundation has no targetable surface, part, internal
  point, geometry or dormant placeholder for one.
- accepted / intelligence boundary: an Agent may understand authored Entity state and
  choose an exact offset, but World neither derives nor validates “two centimetres
  above the tabletop” as a surface distance.
- concrete illustrative input, not canonical schema: if Table T's Position point is
  at its base and the Agent understands its top as 75 centimetres higher, it proposes
  Cup C relative to T with `z = 77 cm`. World stores that explicit basis and offset;
  a later semantic table-shape change moves nothing until an Agent proposes a change.
- scale and failure boundary: the choice adds no per-Entity internal-point collection,
  geometry lookup, descendant rewrite or new hot owner. Stale or misunderstood
  surface meaning can produce a narratively inaccurate offset, but World never claims
  to have mechanically proved the authored surface statement.
- deferred: exact surfaces, Entity-internal points, Orientation and geometry may be
  earned by later concrete gameplay; option A does not add preparatory fields.
- remaining grill frontier: six material User choices remain under the leading path—
  direct topology concept/name, Relation versus exact-fact boundary, Area scope,

## Connection is the dedicated direct Place-topology primitive

- accepted / option A: `Connection` is the canonical one-word name for the separately
  owned mechanical fact that records direct travel direction between two Places.
- rejected / option B: `Link` is not Aicadia vocabulary for this fact; its engine,
  API and hyperlink meanings are less precise than Connection here.
- rejected / option C: Connection is not a server-owned type inside a universal or
  open Relation base. Shared endpoints and a possible combined response do not make
  authored meaning and direct travel one stored truth.
- concrete illustrative state, not canonical schema: A→B makes B one direct option
  returned by a bounded outgoing-Connection read from A. B→A requires its own explicit
  direction. A road Entity or Relation saying “the moss road leads to B” may explain
  the scene but cannot replace or authorize A→B.
- concurrency and scale: duplicate establishment conflicts only on the exact directed
  Place pair and uses no Entity relation count, Place-wide lock, global graph revision
  or Relation scan. Exact database representation remains technical design work.
- next dependency: decide whether open Agent-authored Relation meaning gets one stable
  first-class record or stays only in existing Entity and Position text surfaces.
- remaining frontier: five material User choices remain on the leading path—open
  Relation identity, Area scope, Place qualification/map read, first Movement result
  and remote-effect boundary.
- scope / exploration: vocabulary, Place, Movement, Agent Play, World Change and the
  active backcast carry the choice. No `game/docs`, schema, capability, runtime or
  public Agent text changed.

## Open Relation has one stable non-Entity identity

- accepted / option A: Relation is one stable non-Entity World record directed from
  one source Entity to one target Entity, with a free Agent-authored name and
  description and no server-owned semantic kind or mechanical authority.
- accepted / multiplicity: several Relations may coexist between the same Entity
  pair. Prose similarity never merges them, and changing Relation wording retains its
  exact stable identity rather than making free text the natural key.
- accepted / history and authority: Activity can identify the exact Relation involved.
  Original authorship remains attributable history, not permanent exclusive edit
  control over ordinary current Relation meaning.
- concrete illustrative state, not canonical schema: Relation R1 directs Dog D toward
  Bridge B with name “sleeps under” and a free description. Developing the wording
  addresses R1; another independent D→B Relation may coexist. World neither derives
  Position nor grants movement, inventory, control or remote effects from either.
- rejected / option B: `(source, target, free name)` is not Relation identity because
  harmless wording changes would delete and recreate the fact and synonymous names
  would become accidental concurrency and duplicate rules.
- rejected / option C: embedding endpoint meaning only in Trait or Position text
  would prevent deterministic endpoint validation, reverse lookup and exact history
  without forbidden prose interpretation.
- scale and failure boundary: exact Relation writes share no endpoint count, lock or
  revision. Reads require endpoint, direction, cursor and limit; a hot endpoint may
  still create real index pressure, but never authorizes an unbounded graph read.
- deferred technical details: exact establish/develop operations, ordinary text
  bounds, dependency revisions, privacy and duplicate presentation. No production
  schema is accepted by the illustrative record.
- remaining frontier: four material User choices remain—Area scope, Place
  qualification/map read, first Movement result and remote-effect boundary.
- scope / exploration: vocabulary, Place, Agent Play, Multiplayer, World Change,

## Area options withdrawn; Connection traversal context added

- challenged / all proposed options: the User selected none of deferring Area,

- scale and failure boundary: an answer must keep geometry or traversal reads bounded,

## Place extent and Connection traversal research completed

- researched / no product choice: the focused
  [primary-source report](../../research/place-area-connection-traversal.md) compares
  Place extent, spatial inclusion or overlap and ordered Connection traversal as
  three independent truths across OGC, PostGIS, Overture, Detour and Unreal sources.
- established constraint: Connection endpoint topology cannot determine intervening
  landscape. The same A→B endpoints can describe many courses or a portal with no
  ordinary intervening space; a straight endpoint line would invent false World state.
- candidate 1: an exact oriented course plus current extent intersections derives an
  ordered multiset of crossings. It follows boundary changes but requires valid,
  compatible geometry and bounded revision-specific spatial work.
- candidate 2: Agent-authored structured intervals give an ordered traversal without
  complete geometry and can represent impossible topology. They require their own
  lifecycle and may disagree with later Area boundaries.
- rejected inference: storing both candidates as equally current truth is not a free
  hybrid. A deterministic authority rule is required, and materialized derived
  intersections risk high invalidation fan-out after one large boundary change.
- scale boundary: spatial indexes and shape subdivision limit candidate work but not
  result count. Repeated and simultaneous crossings are legitimate; reads require
  course-position pagination, stable tie-breaking and exact input revisions rather
  than a global map revision or silent truncation.
- replacement frontier: choose the source of ordinary Connection traversal context,
  then Area extent/inclusion semantics. Under the leading path, five material User
  choices remain before technical synthesis.
- source audit: all 30 unique primary links returned HTTP 200; documentation lint and
  scoped whitespace validation succeeded.
- scope / research only: report, research index, active backcast, Place, Movement and
  backlog carry the findings. No `dev/CONTEXT.md`, `game/docs`, schema, capability,
  runtime or public Agent text changed.

- shape clarification: ordered points/line, corridor, structured intervals, completed

## Connection selected as one stable travel alternative

- accepted / ownership: one Connection is one stable direct travel alternative, not

## Ordered Connection points and optional shape description accepted

- accepted / exact representation: a Connection's optional reusable spatial shape

- meaning boundary: the points describe one exact representative course for that

- concurrency and scale: one shape change conflicts on its exact Connection revision;

- deferred technical details: point and integer bounds, endpoint alignment,

## Area accepted as exact positive coverage

- accepted / Area meaning: established Area geometry proves that covered points fall
  in its Place. A point outside all current coverage remains unknown rather than
  proven outside; missing Area means no coverage is established.
- overlap consequence: several Place Areas may positively cover the same point.
  World never selects a universal parent, merges Place identities or infers movement,
  visibility or ownership from that overlap.
- derived traversal: intersecting current Connection segments with eligible Areas
  yields proven ordered portions plus unknown gaps. Intersections are calculated
  output, not independent current crossing rows.
- concurrency and scale: an Area change conflicts on the exact Place Area and causes
  no Connection-wide rewrite. Spatial-index candidate reads, explicit revisions,
  stable ordering, cursor and limit bound one result without a global map revision.
- rejected foundation: Area is neither an obligatorily complete inside/outside
  boundary nor descriptive-only geometry that World cannot query deterministically.
- deferred technical details: exact geometry representation, disconnected and
  vertical coverage, corrections, operations and visibility.
- remaining frontier: three leading choices remain—Place qualification/map reads,
  first Movement result and remote-effect boundaries.
- scope / exploration: Area vocabulary, Place, Movement, Multiplayer, SP08, active
  backcast and backlog carry the choice. No `game/docs`, production schema,
  capability, runtime or public Agent text changed.

## Deliberate Place role and bounded map window accepted

- accepted / Place qualification: any positioned Entity may deliberately receive
  the Place role when Agent and User establish it as an independent spatial
  reference. World has no semantic kind allowlist and Position never grants the role
  automatically.
- one-subject identity: Place remains a role keyed by `entity_id`; Entity retains
  name, description, Properties and Traits, while Place adds Position requirement,
  optional Area and Connection participation. Accepted establishment leaves Activity.
- accepted / map consequence: one exact bounded World window selects Places by
  resolved Position or known positive Area intersection and can return bounded Place,
  Area and touching-Connection context with stable continuation.
- clarified vocabulary: the coordinate-window map read is not Place neighborhood,
  which follows explicit structural relationships from one exact Place. Neither read
  returns every positioned Entity or implies visibility by itself.
- concurrency and scale: Place establishment touches only the exact Entity. Spatial
  indexes bound candidates, cursors and limits bound hot-region results and no global
  Place count, map revision or lock exists.
- rejected: automatic Place promotion for every Position, a server-owned allowed-kind
  list and an unbounded all-Place map response.
- deferred technical details: exact window form, limits, cursor shape, visibility,
  establishment operation and public interface names.
- remaining frontier: two leading choices remain—the first Movement result and the
  remote-effect boundary.
- scope / exploration: Place vocabulary, Place, Agent Play, Multiplayer, active
  backcast and backlog carry the choice. No `game/docs`, production schema,
  capability, runtime or public Agent text changed.

## Partial-or-complete Connection Movement accepted

- accepted / player outcome: one Movement may traverse a whole shaped Connection or
  stop at one exact whole-centimetre point on an explicitly named segment. The
  intermediate result remains an ordinary Position and never requires a Place.
- exact proposal: the Agent names Connection, segment, target point, allowed
  direction and expected Connection and Character Position revisions. World validates
  typed structure and never derives a destination from prose.
- no journey state: accepted Movement changes Character Position and stores Activity
  atomically. It creates no current Connection membership, progress, timer,
  background process or completed Position trace; every later step revalidates.
- endpoint behavior: a shapeless portal permits only direct endpoint travel. At an
  ordinary arrival the Agent supplies one complete Position and may explicitly
  re-reference the Character to the destination Place; World never does so silently.
- concurrency and scale: travellers read the shared Connection and write independent
  Position rows. No traveller count or course lock makes a popular road serialize
  its Users; a true Connection revision change rejects stale dependent proposals.
- rejected foundation: mandatory immediate arrival and a durable timed journey are
  both unnecessary for the selected first model.
- deferred technical details: exact arithmetic, segment and endpoint alignment,
  limits, no-op behavior, operation names, errors, observation and later timed travel.
- remaining frontier: one material choice remains—the remote-effect boundary.
- scope / exploration: Place, Movement, Multiplayer, SP01, SP06, active backcast and
  backlog carry the choice. No `dev/CONTEXT.md`, `game/docs`, production schema,
  capability, runtime or public Agent text changed.

## Executable remote basis rejected in favor of Agent-understood Relation

- rejected / previous recommendation: no new typed remote-control record is added
  merely to make Button B detonate Bomb X. The User judged that foundation too
  ceremonial before concrete protected gameplay requires it.
- accepted / Relation role: an eligible open B→X Relation may give the Agent causal
  meaning and may be named as current context, but it never executes the effect,
  grants authority or bypasses ordinary World validation.
- exact action: after User preview and confirmation, the Agent submits B, X, every
  expected revision and exact bounded mutation. World may validate a claimed Relation
  identity, endpoints and revision but never its semantic wording.
- settlement: ordinary subject eligibility, authority, freshness, idempotency and
  bounds decide the whole Action. Accepted state and Activity settle atomically;
  Relation cannot rescue an otherwise unauthorized or stale Bomb change.
- spatial boundary: distance, Position, Area, Connection and Route neither grant nor
  block causality. They ground current subjects and occurrence context only; World
  infers no radius, victims, chain, physics or delayed work.
- known limitation: User confirmation and ordinary authority do not prove semantic
  correctness against a mistaken or modified Agent. Future concrete protected or
  high-impact gameplay may earn a typed authorization mechanism then.
- scale: one request validates exact Button, optional Relation context, Bomb and
  explicitly affected subjects. There is no Place scan, global rule row, background
  Agent, fan-out or unrelated lock.
- grill status: the spatial product-choice frontier is empty. Remaining geometry,
  operations, limits, concurrency, privacy, public interface and evidence work is
  technical synthesis and planning, not accepted implementation.
- scope / exploration: Agent Play, Multiplayer S03, World Change, SP03, active
  backcast and backlog carry the choice. No `dev/CONTEXT.md`, `game/docs`, production
  schema, capability, runtime or public Agent text changed.

## Spatial technical synthesis completed without implementation authority

- completed / recommendation: the accepted spatial choices now translate into one
  falsifiable technical candidate: Entity identity with separate sparse Position,
  Place, Area, Connection and Relation truths, per-truth immutable versions and
  current revisions, typed Activity dependencies and disposable indexes.
- Position candidate: Position keeps Entity identity, whole-centimetre `BIGINT`
  offsets from World or one reference Entity, one optional description and an
  Activity-backed current pointer. A bounded recursive resolution plus stable
  dependency locks and checked integer arithmetic is the leading PostgreSQL shape;
  exact lock modes and coordinate bounds require evidence.
- traversal candidate: Connection keeps immutable Place endpoints and versioned
  directions, text and optional ordered World points. Movement validates one exact
  target point and revision, updates only the Character Position and current Place
  when arriving, and creates no journey or traveller state.
- Relation candidate: Relation keeps immutable Entity endpoints and versioned free
  name and description without kind, Properties or executable payload. Activity may
  cite its exact revision as causal context, while ordinary Action authority remains
  independently decisive; self-directed authored meaning remains structurally valid.
- Area boundary: no placeholder geometry enters the first schema. One Place-keyed
  current positive coverage with immutable versions is the leading storage boundary;
  bounded horizontal polygon components with explicit vertical intervals are the
  preferred first geometry candidate, not an accepted representation.
- scale: quiet Position writes touch no region or World row; hot-point reads page
  without a count; hot Connection travellers share only a short read dependency;
  carrier moves change one canonical Position; and relative-Place map indexing stays
  behind a false-negative and dirty-rebuild proof gate.
- delivery order: first prove Position lineage and concurrency in a productionward
  real-PostgreSQL lab, then plan the smallest direct-Position second-Place discovery
  and Connection Movement slice. Relative Position, Relation and Area follow only as
  independently earned complete behavior.
- status: technical synthesis is complete, but no plan has been accepted and no
  `game/docs`, migration, World behavior, HTTP, MCP, runtime or public Agent surface
  is authorized or changed.
- scope / exploration: the active five-year spatial backcast owns the full technical
  candidate; Place, Movement, Multiplayer, World Change and backlog point to it
  without duplicating a production contract.

# 2026-08-20

## PostgreSQL Position-lineage candidate survives its bounded lab

- accepted / execution: the User accepted the retained real-PostgreSQL Position
  plan. Its fixed eight-case fixture was implemented without changing production
  schema, `game/docs`, World behavior, HTTP, MCP or public Agent text.
- supported / lineage and arithmetic: direct and 64-level relative Position resolved
  exactly; symmetric coordinate failure wrote no partial Activity, version or
  pointer; exact sequential retry returned one original Activity.
- supported / concurrency: a synchronized A→B/B→A re-reference race committed
  exactly one new Position and left both chains acyclic. `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` Entity
  coordination remained compatible with the fixture's foreign-key participation,
  while a `FOR SHARE` Position dependency blocked the conflicting writer.
- supported / carrier boundary: moving one Ship added one Ship Position version and
  one Activity while exactly 1,000 relative descendants received no move version or
  Activity. Cabin-local state committed under the held Ship writer; an operation
  requiring Cabin's external point waited and resolved through the new Ship point
  only after commit.
- observed / bounded timeout: both deliberate waits returned PostgreSQL `57014`
  because the equal 150 ms statement timeout won before a distinct lock-timeout
  code. The lab proves the intended bounded conflict in its fixture, not a public
  retry result.
- evidence boundary: the kept lab does not prove production migrations, World
  authority, privacy, typed Activity dependencies, concurrent first positioning,
  PostGIS or map indexing, failover, load or million-User capacity. Its code may not
  be promoted or imported into runtime code.
- retained / complete spatial direction: this result narrows only the Position
  under-layer. The active synthesis and Areas continue to retain the separate Place,
  Area, Connection, Relation, Movement, map-read, remote-causality and later privacy
  boundaries; the lab neither replaces nor forgets that full system.
- forward state: the first complete direct-Position second-Place discovery and
  Connection Movement slice remains the preferred next productionward edge, but it
  still requires a separate accepted plan and contract work.

## Complete spatial delivery planning opened as staged slices

- proposed / planning structure: one draft plan now owns an executable first spatial
  slice and preserves S2–S6 as dependency gates, instead of keeping one multi-year
  mega-build active or pretending direct Position is the complete system.
- proposed / S1 outcome: one bounded eligible map read, confirmed new-or-reused Place
  expansion with a stable Connection while the Character remains at A, followed by
  separately confirmed complete or partial Movement with exact Position and Activity.
- retained / complete scope: relative carriers, open Relations, positive Area,
  private coat/inventory arrangements, named Routes, terrain mechanics, protected
  remote control and opt-in unforeseen results remain visible as independent later
  slices that require their own contracts, plans and evidence.
- scenario coverage: the draft maps all fixed SP01–SP15 cases to the first slice or
  their owning later gate so no unresolved coat, bomb, cup, dog, hotel, ship, forest,
  map, privacy or impossible-topology pressure is lost.
- draft blockers: map eligibility, Discovery admission, Connection deduplication,
  public capability boundaries, observation away from a Place, Activity vocabulary
  and representability/request bounds still require negotiation before activation.
- status boundary: this is a proposed delivery structure only. No backlog edge,
  `game/docs`, migration, World behavior, HTTP, MCP or public Agent text is selected,
  authorized or changed by drafting it.

## Spatial map begins with Character knowledge, not coordinate omniscience

- accepted / first direction: a bounded map window never returns every established
  Place merely because its Position falls inside the requested coordinates. World
  first selects Places the acting Character can deterministically know, then applies
  spatial filtering and pagination.
- accepted / discovery consequence: the exact destination and Connection accepted
  through that Character's Place Discovery become knowable to it even though
  Discovery leaves the Character at its origin. Coordinate proximity reveals no
  additional Places and Connection knowledge never recursively exposes the World.
- concrete player result: Mara may see current A and personally discovered B in her
  map window while hidden C in the same window is absent. Another Character does not
  receive B merely because Mara discovered it or because it guesses B's coordinates.
- corrected / implementation optimism: arbitrary Activity participation cannot be
  treated as current Knowledge because Participation has a different meaning and
  current eligibility may not be reconstructed generically from history.
- open / next dependency: choose the smallest durable current basis—leading option
  is one sparse Character–Place knowledge association written atomically by exact
  accepted entry, Discovery, arrival and later explicit introduction. Transmission,
  revocation and private-Place rules remain unchosen.
- scale and security: eligibility lookup is Character-scoped and spatial reads remain
  bounded and paginated; no global map revision, Place audience array, recursive
  Connection walk or guessed-window enumeration is introduced.
- scope / draft planning: spatial plan, Place, Discovery, Agent Play and Multiplayer
  Areas carry the direction. No backlog selection, `game/docs`, migration, World,
  HTTP, MCP or public Agent text changed.

## Sparse Character–Place knowledge selected; UserSeen semantics rejected

- accepted / option A: current Place knowability uses one sparse durable association
  per exact Character–Place pair. It is established only by explicit accepted World
  behavior, not inferred from coordinates, arbitrary Activity or Connection reachability.
- clarified / owner: Knowledge belongs to the Character Entity, not the controlling
  User or Agent. Current one-Character-per-User cardinality does not make `user_id`
  the correct in-World owner.
- clarified / read boundary: a map or other read creates no knowledge write. A
  `seen_at` field would describe Observation/read history rather than the selected
  current eligibility; Activity already owns when and through which accepted act the
  association was established.
- concrete technical candidate, not accepted schema: a Place-typed row can use
  natural identity `(character_entity_id, place_entity_id)` plus one establishing
  Activity foreign key. Entry and accepted Place Discovery insert it in their state
  transaction; repeat establishment is a no-op rather than another row or counter.
- challenged / generic subject table: `(subject_type, subject_id)` cannot natively
  foreign-key different Entity, Place, Connection and Relation targets, and “knows
  this Entity” does not prove which role or fact is known. A generic Knowledge base
  with typed extensions remains possible but has not earned its extra identity,
  tables or lifecycle from the one current Place-map scenario.
- scale: storage grows with actually known Character–Place pairs; primary-key and
  Character-first indexes bound map eligibility. No audience array, per-read write,
  global counter or hot Place knowledge row exists.
- open / next dependency: choose Place-typed natural identity for S1 or a generic
  Knowledge base with typed targets; then decide the exact accepted acts that first
  establish the association and whether Connection knowledge needs its own typed row.
- scope / vocabulary and draft planning: canonical Knowledge, spatial plan, Place,
  Discovery, Agent Play and Multiplayer Areas are aligned. No backlog selection,
  `game/docs`, migration, World, HTTP, MCP or public Agent text changed.

## Laravel-style polymorphic Knowledge confirmed feasible; target integrity remains open

- corrected / performance claim: a polymorphic table is not inherently slow or
  unserious. A Character-leading composite primary key
  `(character_entity_id, subject_type, subject_id)` can provide bounded lookup,
  non-null identity and concurrent uniqueness for the current access pattern.
- clarified / Laravel comparison: Laravel's convention supplies a type/id pair and
  target-first non-unique index; stable aliases decouple stored types from class
  names. Aicadia would still own its Character key, uniqueness, lifecycle, bounds
  and referential-integrity choice.
- corrected / current consumers: S1 already makes two exact subject families
  knowable—the accepted destination Place and the accepted Connection—so a shared
  row shape no longer lacks a second concrete consumer. This does not admit Entity,
  Relation or arbitrary future types now.
- preserved / semantic boundary: one Knowledge row would mean only that this
  Character may address that subject through its typed authorized read. It never
  proves that every Position, Property, Relation or other fact about the subject is
  known.
- preserved / database boundary: no PostgreSQL foreign key can dynamically select
  the Place or Connection table from `subject_type`. A composite index solves lookup
  and duplicate admission, not target existence. Deterministic typed World
  validation or separate typed association tables remain the live alternatives.
- current preference, not accepted: one polymorphic row limited to stable `place`
  and `connection` aliases, with Character owner and establishing Activity, is now
  preferred because both current subjects share establishment and provenance. The
  User must still choose whether World-enforced target integrity is sufficient or
  native target foreign keys justify two tables.
- evidence: [Laravel-style polymorphic Character knowledge storage](../../research/polymorphic-character-knowledge-storage.md).
- scope: the active concept, spatial draft plan and relevant Areas carry this
  correction. No `game/docs`, schema, runtime, HTTP, MCP or public Agent contract is
  authorized or changed.

## Knowledge creation time selected; subject-update fan-out rejected

- accepted / direct time: each current Knowledge association carries an immutable,
  server-authored `created_at` written in the same transaction as the accepted act
  which first establishes it for that Character.
- clarified / meaning: `created_at` means first Knowledge establishment, not always
  Discovery. Entry, arrival or a later accepted introduction may establish it too;
  the linked Activity retains the exact cause and historical context.
- preserved / no read receipt: reading a map or subject never changes `created_at`
  and creates no Knowledge write.
- rejected / subject fan-out: changing a Place or Connection does not update every
  Character's Knowledge row. Current subject state and version remain on the subject;
  audience-wide Knowledge writes would be semantically false and create unbounded
  amplification on a popular subject.
- open / `updated_at`: current Knowledge has no accepted mutable field or lifecycle,
  so no meaningful update timestamp is yet selected. Re-learning, verification,
  forgetting and revocation remain unchosen behaviors rather than implicit writes.
- current preference, not accepted: omit `updated_at` until one concrete accepted
  Knowledge mutation gives it a precise meaning; otherwise it is dead data equal to
  `created_at`.
- scope: the active concept and spatial draft plan carry the choice. No canonical
  vocabulary, `game/docs`, schema, runtime or public contract changed.

## Repeated Character views proposed as Observation occurrences

- proposed / player value: each genuine repeat encounter with a Place, Entity or
  later eligible subject may remain an immutable personal occurrence, letting an
  Agent ground recognition, recurrence and “when did I last encounter this?” in
  actual Character history rather than one overwritten timestamp.
- clarified / existing vocabulary: this candidate is Observation, already defined
  as information one Character acquires through one situated encounter or accepted
  observation path. Knowledge remains current justified information; Observation is
  occurrence history and not a second authoritative copy of the subject.
- preserved / transport boundary: ordinary World, HTTP or MCP reads never create an
  Observation. Allowing `get` retries or map refreshes to write would let a User
  manufacture encounters and unbounded history without an in-World act.
- preserved / World boundary: World cannot infer “saw” from prose, distance, names
  or Agent judgment. Any Observation occurrence needs one explicit idempotent act,
  exact observer and bounded subjects plus structural eligibility World can validate.
- preserved / scale and privacy: no stored `view_count`, global reverse observer
  query, background Agent call or subject-to-all-observers fan-out is introduced.
  Repeated occurrences would append only for the acting Character's accepted act.
- open / first dependency: decide which concrete action counts as Observation before
  deciding whether immutable Activity participation is sufficient or a separate
  Observation record has earned storage. Current Movement arrival, explicit direct
  Interaction and a later dedicated observation act are distinct candidates.
- corrected / timestamps: `created_at` on current Knowledge still records first
  establishment. `updated_at` is no longer the preferred way to preserve every
  encounter; whether a last-observed projection is useful depends on the accepted
  occurrence model.
- scope: the active concept, spatial draft plan and Multiplayer Area carry the
  proposal. No `dev/CONTEXT.md` change is needed because Observation already exists;
  no `game/docs`, schema, runtime or public Agent contract changed.

## Explicit Observation and per-occurrence Agent text selected

- accepted / option A: an Observation occurrence comes only from an explicit,
  idempotent accepted in-World observation path with exact observer and bounded
  eligible subjects. An ordinary World/HTTP/MCP read, retry or map refresh never
  creates one; prose alone cannot prove it happened.
- clarified / technical split: Knowledge is one current Character–subject
  association supporting fast subject eligibility. Observation is immutable
  occurrence history, so the same Character may accumulate several Observations of
  the same Place, Entity, Character or later eligible subject without duplicating
  current World state.
- accepted / authored detail: each Observation may carry bounded free text from the
  observing Character's Agent, such as “this time he wore a red hat.” World validates
  actor, subject, structural eligibility, request identity, bounds and atomic
  Activity but does not interpret that note.
- preserved / no implicit mutation: observation text alone creates no Hat Entity,
  wearing Relation, Trait, Property, Position or change to the observed Character.
  Structured current World truth remains separately established and validated.
- scale and retry: one new intentional observation may append one bounded set of
  occurrence records; the same idempotent request appends none. Reads remain bounded
  and no stored count, reverse observer list, subject fan-out or background Agent
  invocation is introduced.
- open / note authority: decide whether the free text is private, attributed memory
  of the observing Character—which may be selective or mistaken—or shared World
  canon visible through the subject. The current preference is private memory;
  shared facts should use their ordinary exact World structures.
- vocabulary: `dev/CONTEXT.md` now distinguishes Observation as one acquisition
  occurrence from Knowledge as durable availability across occurrences. No new term
  was introduced.
- scope: the active concept, spatial draft plan, Agent Play and Multiplayer Areas
  carry this direction. No `game/docs`, schema, runtime or public contract changed.

## Observation account belongs privately to the observing Character

- accepted / option A: bounded free text on an Observation is a private, attributed
  account belonging to the observing Character. It may be selective, interpretive
  or wrong and remains useful as memory without becoming shared World canon.
- concrete consequence: Mara's note “this time he wore a red hat” neither creates a
  Hat Entity nor proves, equips or exposes one on Ivo. A shared worn hat requires its
  separately accepted exact Entity and structural World state.
- authorization: the note is available only through Mara's authorized Character
  context. Reading Ivo does not return Mara's note; Ivo receives no observer list and
  no global reverse query reveals who recorded him.
- World boundary: World validates observer control, exact eligible subjects,
  idempotency, text bounds, occurrence time and atomic Activity but never evaluates
  or reconciles the meaning of the private account against current World truth.
- artistic result: a User-owned Agent may ground callbacks, mistaken recognition and
  evolving motifs in the Character's own past accounts while explicitly presenting
  them as memory rather than fresh authoritative fact.
- open / next dependency: decide whether every accepted Observation atomically
  establishes the one current Knowledge association for each exact observed subject.
  The current preference is yes; repeat Observations append history without
  duplicating Knowledge.
- scope: canonical Observation wording, active concept, spatial draft plan, Agent
  Play and Multiplayer Areas are aligned. No `game/docs`, schema, runtime or public
  contract changed.

## Knowledge parked; distant Observation does not prove recognition

- corrected / no automatic consequence: an accepted Observation never by itself
  establishes Knowledge, recognition or a known identity. The prior preference to
  insert Knowledge atomically from every Observation is rejected.
- accepted / distance case: Mara may observe an unclear figure far away without
  knowing that it is Ivo, receiving Ivo's name or gaining a current Entity read.
  Observation preserves what Mara experienced rather than all identity World knows.
- parked / Knowledge: Knowledge storage, establishment, subject types and hydration
  are deliberately paused. The earlier sparse Character-owned direction remains
  design history but no Observation choice may silently complete that branch; the
  spatial plan remains draft and this is still an implementation blocker.
- preserved / private account: free Observation text remains Character-owned,
  possibly mistaken memory and never mutates or identifies the observed subject.
- accepted / exact Observation target: every Observation references exactly one
  existing model record through required subject type and id. Subjectless Observation
  is rejected even when the Character-facing account describes an unknown figure.
- clarified / no identity grant: the exact internal reference lets World match
  repeated occurrences but does not automatically expose the subject's name, hydrate
  current fields, prove recognition or establish Knowledge.
- technical consequence: target type dispatch and existence validation are
  deterministic World work because the polymorphic reference cannot use one dynamic
  PostgreSQL foreign key. The exact current admitted model-type set remains open;
  arbitrary runtime class and table names are not accepted by implication.
- scale and security: the exact target supports bounded subject-indexed recurrence
  without a Knowledge write, subject fan-out, reverse observer query or stored count.
  Ordinary reads still create no Observation.
- scope: canonical Observation wording, active concept, draft spatial plan, Agent
  Play and Multiplayer Areas are aligned. No `game/docs`, schema, runtime or public
  contract changed.

## Explicit expandable Observation model list selected

- accepted / option A: Observation uses a World-owned explicit list of stable model
  aliases. Current admission is exactly `entity`, `place` and `connection`; arbitrary
  runtime class names, table names and unknown strings are rejected.
- clarified / model roles: a Character, animal, plant or object is observed as
  `entity`; a city, forest or other Entity observed specifically in its spatial
  reference role is `place`; one exact direct travel alternative is `connection`.
- rejected / unnecessary targets now: Position, Property and Trait are eligible
  details of their owning Entity or Place rather than separate Observation subjects.
  Relation, Area and future models remain absent until accepted behavior earns an
  additive type.
- technical and scale consequence: World has one exhaustive three-way dispatch for
  target existence and authorization. Stable aliases survive code refactors; bounded
  observer- and subject-leading indexes never depend on prose or runtime reflection.
- preserved / privacy: required subject type and id do not establish Knowledge,
  recognition or target hydration. The private account may still call the referenced
  Entity “an unknown figure.”
- open / next dependency: choose whether each Observation needs its own dedicated
  operation, or may be an explicit optional result inside a structurally grounding
  Movement, Interaction or other accepted action. Automatic inference and API-read
  writes remain rejected.
- scope: active concept, draft spatial plan, Agent Play and Multiplayer Areas carry
  the selected set. No `game/docs`, schema, runtime or public contract changed.

## Operation-embedded Observation selected as reversible first trial

- provisionally accepted / option A: a structurally grounding accepted action may
  explicitly include its Observation result. Movement may record an Observation on
  arrival and Interaction may record an Observation of its exact model subject; the
  Agent supplies it and World never infers it from coordinates, prose or action type.
- concrete boundary: ordinary World, HTTP, MCP and map reads still create no
  Observation. A future pure-look behavior may receive a dedicated Observation
  operation rather than pretending to be Movement or Interaction.
- atomicity and retry: the grounding state change, Activity and Observation rows
  settle in one transaction. A failed action writes none; the same idempotent retry
  appends no duplicate occurrence.
- reversible / test intent: this is the first combined flow to try, not a generic
  action-effects abstraction or an irrevocable public contract. If concrete payloads
  or confirmations become unclear, a dedicated Observation operation remains the
  clean fallback without changing the chosen Observation model.
- technical and scale consequence: each operation admits only its exact bounded
  explicit Observation input. It creates no inferred fan-out, background work,
  subject counter or hot shared observer row; conflicts remain on the action's exact
  dependencies and observer-owned occurrence writes.
- open / next dependency: decide whether one grounding action may explicitly append
  several independently targeted Observations or exactly one. No exact list bound or
  schema has been chosen.
- scope: active concept, draft spatial plan, Agent Play and Multiplayer Areas carry
  the provisional direction. No `game/docs`, schema, runtime or public contract
  changed; `dev/CONTEXT.md` needs no change because Observation meaning is unchanged.

## Several Observations per grounding action selected

- accepted / option A: one grounding action may explicitly append several separate
  Observation occurrences. Each occurrence retains exactly one admitted model
  reference and its own optional bounded private account; all share the action's
  Activity and atomic idempotent settlement.
- concrete example: one Movement through a sandy Connection into Duindorp may record
  one Connection Observation and one Place Observation. It does not combine both
  references into one ambiguous Observation.
- rejected: exactly one Observation per action would require artificial follow-up
  actions, confirmations and Activities for one experienced moment. One Observation
  with several model references would weaken the already selected exact-subject
  meaning and complicate authorization and later retrieval.
- atomicity and retry: action failure leaves no partial Observation set; retrying the
  same request appends none. The collection is explicit and World neither infers nor
  fans out additional occurrences.
- scale boundary: the request will receive one finite technical admission limit with
  the other S1 bounds so a single transaction cannot be unbounded. That limit is not
  yet selected, is no gameplay score and places no total cap on retained Character
  history. No subject counter or globally shared observer row is touched.
- open / next dependency: decide which exact Entity, Place or Connection references
  each grounding action may structurally admit. Cardinality alone grants no right to
  name arbitrary existing or guessed subjects.
- scope: active concept, draft spatial plan, Agent Play and Multiplayer Areas carry
  the choice. No `game/docs`, schema, runtime or public contract changed;
  `dev/CONTEXT.md` needs no change because Observation meaning is unchanged.

## Movement Observation subjects limited to its exact authorized result

- accepted / option A: Movement may admit as Observation subjects only its exact
  traversed Connection, destination Place and Entities included in World's bounded
  authorized arrival result for the acting Character. The Agent explicitly selects
  a subset; World never creates the occurrences automatically.
- concrete example: if the result contains Connection 7, Duindorp and a dog on the
  square, the Agent may record Duindorp and the dog as two Observations. A sword in
  another Character's hidden inventory is absent and cannot be admitted by guessing
  its identifier.
- deterministic boundary: World validates exact typed membership and the current
  structural versions grounding that result. It does not parse Observation text,
  infer visibility or equate the result with every Entity at the Place.
- failure behavior: one submitted subject outside the admitted result rejects the
  entire atomic proposal. Silently dropping the invalid Observation while accepting
  a different Movement than the User confirmed is rejected.
- scale and privacy: arrival selection stays Character-authorized and bounded even
  at a deliberately hot Place. It creates no global visibility set, Entity counter,
  reverse observer query or fan-out; guessed identifiers reveal nothing.
- open / newly exposed dependency: an Agent cannot author an informed private account
  of a newly encountered Entity until World returns it, while the provisional atomic
  embedded design places Observation input before Movement settlement. The next
  choice must resolve this timing without pretending the Agent knew arrival state in
  advance.
- scope: active concept, draft spatial plan, Agent Play and Multiplayer Areas carry
  the choice. No `game/docs`, schema, runtime or public contract changed;
  `dev/CONTEXT.md` needs no change because no domain term changed.

## Post-Movement Observation supersedes embedded atomic recording

- accepted / option B: Movement settles first, writes its own Activity and returns
  the acting Character's bounded authorized arrival result. The Agent may only then
  issue a separate explicit Observation action grounded by that Movement.
- superseded: the provisional option A in which Agent-authored Observation text and
  Movement settled in one transaction is no longer current. It required the Agent to
  describe newly returned Entities before it could receive them.
- corrected cardinality: one follow-up Observation action may still write several
  occurrences, each with one exact model and private account. They share that
  recording action's Activity and refer to the grounding Movement Activity; they do
  not share Movement's transaction.
- failure and retry: the Observation batch plus its Activity settle atomically and
  idempotently. Failure stores no partial batch but never rolls back, repeats or
  invalidates the already accepted Movement.
- preserved eligibility: only the Movement's exact Connection, destination Place and
  Entities in its bounded authorized arrival result are candidates. An absent or
  guessed subject rejects the Observation action, not Movement, and no invalid item
  is silently dropped.
- scale consequence: two short bounded writes replace one transaction that depended
  on Agent knowledge from the future. There is no long database transaction, global
  lock, server Agent invocation, automatic Observation or hot subject counter.
- open / next dependency: decide how any World instance validates exact earlier
  arrival-result membership during the follow-up without permanently storing every
  returned candidate merely in case it is later observed.
- scope: active concept, draft spatial plan, Agent Play and Multiplayer Areas are
  corrected. No `game/docs`, schema, runtime or public contract changed;
  `dev/CONTEXT.md` needs no change because Observation meaning is unchanged.

## Agent-owned Observation selection supersedes arrival-result proof

- corrected / User rejects A, B and C: World does not permanently store Movement
  candidates, issue a temporary arrival proof or re-read a current scene to decide
  what the Character saw. Those options misplaced semantic intelligence in World.
- accepted direction: World tools return bounded structurally authorized information;
  the explicitly invoked User-owned Agent interprets that context, decides what its
  Character could observe and authors the exact Observation subjects and private
  accounts through an Observation action.
- corrected Movement dependency: Movement may naturally precede Observation but is
  not a required grounding receipt or visibility authority. The earlier option B is
  retained only as a possible player-flow ordering; required Movement linkage and
  exact arrival-result membership proof are superseded.
- preserved storage: one Observation action may write several one-model occurrences
  and its own Activity atomically and idempotently. Recording failure cannot undo a
  prior Movement, and ordinary reads still create no Observation automatically.
- privacy boundary: tools must still omit information the Character is not
  structurally authorized to receive, such as another Character's hidden inventory.
  The Agent cannot reason from absent content; guessing an id grants no current read
  or mutation. Observation itself does not become a server visibility engine.
- rejected infrastructure: no visibility candidate table, arrival snapshot, sealed
  receipt, expiry policy, semantic sight rule or global scene recheck is introduced.
- scale consequence: only explicitly chosen bounded Observation rows are written;
  Movement creates no per-candidate write amplification, token or cross-instance
  coordination. A hot Place produces no automatic observer fan-out.
- open / next dependency: decide the smallest deterministic checks World applies to
  the Agent-selected exact model references while leaving sight, attention and prose
  meaning entirely with the Agent.
- scope: active concept, draft spatial plan, Agent Play and Multiplayer Areas are
  corrected. No `game/docs`, schema, runtime or public contract changed;
  `dev/CONTEXT.md` needs no change because Observation meaning remains accurate.

## Minimal deterministic Observation validation selected

- accepted / option A: World validates only authenticated control of the observing
  Character, an admitted `entity`, `place` or `connection` alias, exact target
  existence, bounded batch and account text, idempotency and atomic Observation plus
  Activity storage.
- rejected semantic checks: World does not validate Position, distance, Connection,
  co-presence, sight, attention, plausibility, prose truth or target revision. Those
  judgments belong to the Agent and its Character account.
- authority boundary: accepting a private Observation returns no current subject
  fields and establishes no Knowledge, read permission or mutation authority. A
  guessed or remembered id therefore cannot be used through Observation to inspect
  or change the subject.
- consistency: target existence preserves the selected rule that every Observation
  concerns one real model. Allowing dangling references is rejected; exact external
  error disclosure remains a later public contract choice.
- scale consequence: one bounded set of typed primary-key checks and observer-owned
  inserts touches no target counter, target revision, global visibility state or
  fan-out. A deliberately famous subject is not a shared write hotspot merely
  because many Characters record private accounts about it.
- open / next dependency: decide whether a private Observation needs its own complete
  preview and explicit User confirmation or whether the Agent may record it inside
  an already explicitly invoked active User turn without another approval.
- scope: active concept, draft spatial plan, Agent Play and Multiplayer Areas carry
  the choice. No `game/docs`, schema, runtime or public contract changed;
  `dev/CONTEXT.md` needs no change because Observation meaning is unchanged.
